<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the reverse logarithmic Kodaira inequality for a surjective connected-fiber morphism $`f:(X,E)\to(Y,D)`$ of smooth projective reduced simple-normal-crossing pairs, with $`\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\nolimits (f^*D)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\nolimits E`$, such that <i>X</i> and every boundary stratum are smooth over $`Y\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\nolimits D`$. Together with logarithmic subadditivity, the inequality gives additivity, including both negative-infinity cases. This resolves Popa's logarithmic additivity conjecture positively in the projective reduced-SNC, stratum-smooth setting.

- **claim-reverse-logarithmic-inequality** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-upper-bound)): Under these hypotheses, $$\label{eq:upper-bound} \kappa(X,L_X)\leq\kappa(Y,L_Y)+\kappa(F,L_F).$$ If either term on the right is $-\infty$, then $H^0(X,mL_X)=0$ for every $m>0$.
  - Explanation: The total logarithmic Kodaira dimension is bounded by the sum of the base and very general fiber dimensions under the stated projective, reduced-boundary and stratum-smoothness hypotheses. A negative-infinity term forces every positive-degree total section space to vanish.
- **claim-logarithmic-additivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-additivity)): For the pairs in Theorem <a href="#thm:upper-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:upper-bound">1</a>, $$\label{eq:additivity} \kappa(X,K_X+E)=\kappa(Y,K_Y+D)+\kappa(F,K_F+E_F).$$
  - Explanation: The reverse inequality and the companion logarithmic subadditivity theorem give equality, with the same conventions for negative infinity.
- **claim-base-section-with-prescribed-zero** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-section-construction)): Let $f,E,D$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:upper-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:upper-bound">1</a>, and let $0\ne s\in H^0(X,mL_X)$ for some $m>0$. Then $\kappa(Y,L_Y)\geq0$. If $\kappa(Y,L_Y)=0$ and $s$ vanishes identically on $X_y$ for some $y\in V$, there are an integer $a>0$ and a nonzero $\tau\in H^0(Y,aL_Y)$ with $\tau(y)=0$.
  - Explanation: A nonzero total logarithmic pluriform forces base nonvanishing. When the base logarithmic Kodaira dimension is zero, vanishing on a fiber over any point of the original open base yields a nonzero base pluriform vanishing at that point.
- **claim-logarithmic-hodge-vector** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-logarithmic-vector)): There are a dense smooth open $Y^\circ\subset V$, a polarizable pure rational variation $\mathbb V$ on $Y^\circ$ with an invariant lattice and quasi-unipotent boundary monodromy, and a nonzero algebraic map $$\label{eq:log-input-vector} u:\mathcal O_{Y^\circ}(-L_Y)\longrightarrow \mathcal E^d, \qquad \mathcal E^p=\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_F^p(\mathbb V\otimes\mathcal O_{Y^\circ}), \qquad F^{d+1}\mathbb V=0.$$ On every model $(Y',D')$ just described, the rationally identified map and all its Higgs iterates $$\label{eq:log-input-iterates} \theta^j(u):\mathcal O_{Y'}(-L_{Y'})\dashrightarrow \mathcal E^{d-j}\otimes(B')^{\otimes j}$$ have regular coefficients at every prime of $Y'$ in the preceding sense. Here the variation and its Hodge bundles are understood over the common dense open, and a grade outside their range is zero.* *The same statement holds for the homogeneous tensor replacement used in [prop:marked-period](#prop:marked-period). More precisely, if $u_*$ is obtained from $u$ by homogeneous tensor operations of positive degree $a$ and flat Hodge-compatible projections, then $$\label{eq:log-input-tensor} u_*:\mathcal O(-aL_{Y'})\dashrightarrow E_0, \qquad \theta^j(u_*):\mathcal O(-aL_{Y'})\dashrightarrow E_j\otimes(B')^{\otimes j}$$ have the same coefficient property. Here $\mathbb A$ is the resulting pure variation, $p_*$ is its highest nonzero Hodge index, and $E_j=\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_F^{p_*-j}\mathbb A$. Finite levels and a common further unipotent ramification may be used in these assertions.
  - Explanation: The chosen pluriform produces a nonzero highest Hodge vector. Its Higgs iterates retain regular coefficients in the original logarithmic cotangent lattice on every specified higher model. Homogeneous tensor operations and flat Hodge projections preserve this property.
- **claim-weight-projection-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weight-extension)): Suppose $g:Z\to\Delta$ is projective and has the local form <a href="#eq:log-input-semistable-coordinates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:log-input-semistable-coordinates">[eq:log-input-semistable-coordinates]</a>, with $Z$ smooth, reduced special fiber $V_0$, and horizontal boundary $H$. For fixed $i,j$, the relative log-form cohomology $$\mathcal A^{i,j}=R^j g_*\Omega^i_{Z/\Delta}(\log(V_0+H))$$ is locally free and carries a filtration by subbundles extending the weight filtration on its Hodge grade over $\Delta^*$. Its successive quotients are the Hodge-graded unipotent canonical extensions of the corresponding pure weight variations. In particular, a regular class that belongs generically to a weight step projects regularly to that step’s pure weight quotient.
  - Explanation: Semistable relative logarithmic form cohomology extends with locally free weight subbundles and pure Hodge-graded quotients. A regular class generically in a weight step therefore has a regular pure projection.
- **claim-original-frame-zero-test** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-zero-test)): Suppose $\dim Y>0$ and $y\in V$, and suppose the section $s$ in <a href="#eq:log-input-section" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:log-input-section">[eq:log-input-section]</a> vanishes identically on $f^{-1}(y)$. Blow up $y$ on the base if $\dim Y>1$, and let $A$ be its exceptional divisor; if $\dim Y=1$, put $A=\{y\}$. Along a general transverse test at $A$, after ramification of order $e$ divisible by $m$, the coefficients of $u$ in the pulled-back *original* $\mathcal O_Y(-L_Y)$ frame vanish to order at least $e/m$. Every homogeneous replacement $u_*$ of degree $a>0$ vanishes to order at least $ae/m$ in the corresponding original $\mathcal O_Y(-aL_Y)$ frame.
  - Explanation: Fiberwise vanishing survives the cyclic root and pure weight projection as positive vanishing order in the pulled-back original source frame. Homogeneous tensor replacement scales this order by its degree.
- **claim-marked-period-construction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-marked-period)): Let $B^\circ$ be a smooth irreducible complex quasi-projective variety, and let $\mathbb V$ be a polarizable rational pure variation with an invariant integral lattice. Suppose that a line bundle $\mathcal L$ has a generically nonzero algebraic morphism $$u:\mathcal L\longrightarrow F^p\mathbb V_{\mathbb C}, \qquad F^{p+1}\mathbb V_{\mathbb C}=0.$$ There are a positive integer $a$, a homogeneous tensor construction followed by flat Hodge-compatible projections, and a generically nonzero morphism $$\label{eq:marked-vector} u_*:\mathcal L^{\otimes a}\longrightarrow E_0, \qquad E_0=F^{p_*}\mathbb A, \qquad F^{p_*+1}\mathbb A=0,$$ where $\mathbb A$ is a polarized complex pure variation. The operations may first be performed equivariantly at finite monodromy level. They descend to $B^\circ$ after shrinking it. On smooth projective birational models there is a dominant morphism $b:B\to S$ with connected fibers and the following properties.* 1. **Descent.* On dense smooth opens, $\mathbb A$ is the pullback of an actual variation on $S^\circ$. Its connected representation is irreducible and factors through a rational semisimple adjoint group $G$. The full monodromy acts through its projected action on $\mathfrak g=\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}G$. In particular, finite scalar monodromy in the kernel of that action has been eliminated.* 2. **The marks.* There is an algebraic compact-flag section $j_{\mathrm u}$, possibly empty, determined pointwise and equivariantly by $[u_*]$. In flat coordinates the joint marks $$j=(E_0,j_{\mathrm u})$$ take values in a closed product flag orbit $J$ of $G_{\mathbb C}$. The two kinds of marks use disjoint complex simple factors. Every rational simple factor of $G$ has a complex factor acting nontrivially on $J$. The full Lie-period map together with $j_{\mathrm u}$ is generically immersive on $S^\circ$.* 3. **The nef lines and adjoint positivity.* If $S$ is a point, $\mathbb A$ is constant and we set $\mathcal H=\mathcal H_{\mathrm u}=0$. Otherwise $S$ has nef rational line bundles $\mathcal H$ and $\mathcal H_{\mathrm u}$, with $\mathcal H-\mathcal H_{\mathrm u}$ nef. On a smooth unipotent connected-monodromy cover their pullbacks are respectively $$\det E_0+\mathcal H_{\mathrm u} \quad\hbox{and the positive compact-flag line.}$$ The generic curvature rank of $\mathcal H$ is the generic rank of $dj$, and equals $\nu(\mathcal H)$. If $D_0$ denotes the reduced union of boundary components with nonidentity local monodromy on $\mathfrak g$, then $$\label{eq:marked-initial-big} K_S+D_0+c\mathcal H_{\mathrm u}\quad\text{is big for }c\gg0.$$ Furthermore, $$\label{eq:marked-final-big} K_S+b_0\mathcal H\quad\text{is big for }b_0\gg0.$$* *All assertions about extensions are compatible with further smooth projective modifications and pullback at unipotent level. In particular, the iterated Higgs fields of $u_*$ are polynomial tensor expressions in those of $u$; the construction introduces no division by a local function.
  - Explanation: A homogeneous replacement descends to an actual variation on a quotient retaining the full Lie period and compact vector marks. It eliminates the finite scalar kernel, constructs nef marked lines, and supplies logarithmic and boundary-free adjoint bigness.
- **claim-connected-monodromy-structure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-monodromy)): The group $G_c$ is semisimple. At connected monodromy level the Hodge circles normalize $G_c$, are locally conjugate by $G_c(\mathbb R)^+$, and have locally constant action on every $G_c$-invariant tensor space. The horizontal period differential therefore has the form $$\label{eq:marked-period-tangent} t(v)\in\mathfrak g_{c,\mathbb C}^{-1,1}, \qquad \theta(v)=d\rho\bigl(t(v)\bigr), \qquad [t(v),t(w)]=0.$$ The Lie algebra, and every monodromy-stable sum of its rational simple ideals, is a polarized rational integral variation of weight zero. Its Hodge metric makes $x\mapsto-x^*$ a compact conjugation, preserving each complex simple ideal.
  - Explanation: Connected algebraic monodromy is semisimple. Its normalized Hodge grading identifies the horizontal period differential with commuting degree-lowering Lie elements and gives polarized integral Lie variations on the relevant rational ideals.
- **claim-minimal-homogeneous-replacement** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-replacement)): The replacement <a href="#eq:marked-vector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:marked-vector">[eq:marked-vector]</a> can be chosen so that its connected representation is irreducible and its full representation factors through the action on the retained rational adjoint Lie factors. Among all such homogeneous replacements, choose one with the fewest retained rational simple factors. Write $G$ for their product as an adjoint group and $\mathfrak g=\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}G$.* *Every representation factor used in splitting $\mathbb A$ according to a product decomposition of $G$ is a Hodge-compatible summand of tensor constructions on the corresponding Lie variation, up to a constant shift of the Hodge indices. It can be equipped with the metric for which representation adjoints are induced by Lie adjoints. The highest space $E_0$ is the tensor product of the highest spaces of these factors and has a closed projective homogeneous orbit.
  - Explanation: Tensor operations and flat projections retain a nonzero vector while making the connected representation irreducible and removing the scalar kernel of the projected Lie action. A minimizing choice retains the fewest rational simple factors.
- **claim-compact-vector-flags** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-flags)): For the minimizing replacement of [lem:marked-replacement](#lem:marked-replacement) there is a generically algebraic section $j_{\mathrm u}$ with the compact-flag and factor-visibility properties in part <span class="upright">(ii)</span> of [prop:marked-period](#prop:marked-period). Its factors have flat positive definite metrics, and their flag varieties carry positive Plücker lines with invariant metrics. The construction is pointwise equivariant in $[u_*]$.
  - Explanation: Minimality forces instability on invisible factors. The optimal parabolic assignment then produces generically algebraic compact flags determined equivariantly by the projective vector, making every retained rational factor visible.
- **claim-generic-lie-stabilizer-triviality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-generic-lie-stabilizer)): Let a polarized rational Lie variation on a smooth connected complex quasi-projective variety have an invariant integral lattice, semisimple fiber $\mathfrak a$, and connected algebraic monodromy $\operatorname{Int}(\mathfrak a)$. At a Hodge-generic lifted period, a rational Lie automorphism preserving the Hodge filtration is the identity.
  - Explanation: At a Hodge-generic lifted period, a rational Lie automorphism preserving the filtration commutes with connected inner monodromy. Centerlessness forces that automorphism to be the identity.
- **claim-full-period-and-variation-descent** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-period-descent)): There is an algebraic quotient with connected generic fibers which remembers the full integral period of $\mathfrak g$ and the compact marks. Both $\mathfrak g$ and $\mathbb A$ descend as variations on a dense smooth open of the quotient. Its full Lie period and compact marks give a generically immersive joint map in flat coordinates. The same construction is available for any monodromy-stable collection of rational normal factors, at connected level if necessary.
  - Explanation: The full integral Lie period and compact marks define an algebraic quotient with connected generic fibers. Both coefficient variations descend, and the joint differential is generically immersive on the quotient.
- **claim-loglog-metric-mass-and-nefness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-log-metric)): Let $Z$ be a smooth projective variety of dimension $n$, let $\Delta$ be an SNC divisor, and let $L$ be a rational line bundle. Suppose that on $Z^\circ=Z\setminus\Delta$ it has a smooth Hermitian metric with semipositive curvature form $\Theta$, normalized to represent $c_1(L)$. Suppose that in regular nonvanishing local frames of a Cartier multiple of $L$ the metric weights satisfy $$\label{eq:prelim-loglog-bound} \|\varphi(z)\|\leq C\left(1+ \sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\log(-\log\|z_i\|)\right)$$ near every boundary chart $\Delta=\{z_1\cdots z_\ell=0\}$. Then $L$ is nef. For every Kähler form $\eta$ on $Z$ and $0\leq k\leq n$, $$\label{eq:prelim-mass-identity} \int_{Z^\circ}\Theta^k\wedge\eta^{n-k} =c_1(L)^k[\eta]^{n-k}.$$ In particular, $$\label{eq:prelim-curvature-rank} \nu(L)=\max_{z\in Z^\circ}\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}\Theta_z.$$ When the curvature is real analytic, as for the induced Hodge and subspace metrics used below, this maximum is its generic rank.
  - Explanation: Semipositive curvature with the stated two-sided boundary weight control extends to a nef class and retains the full mixed intersection mass. Numerical dimension equals maximal curvature rank; real analyticity identifies that rank with the generic rank.
- **claim-initial-logarithmic-adjoint-bigness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-initial-adjoint)): For the marked-period quotient just constructed, $$K_S+D_0+c\mathcal H_{\mathrm u} \quad\text{is big for all sufficiently large }c.$$
  - Explanation: The immersive full period and compact-mark differential produces a big determinant line that compares with the logarithmic adjoint plus a sufficiently positive compact-mark twist.
- **claim-horizontal-germ-dimension** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-connection-dimension)): Let $p:\mathcal P\to Z$ be an algebraic principal bundle for a connected semisimple complex algebraic group $H$, where $Z$ is smooth and irreducible. Suppose $\mathcal P$ carries an algebraic flat principal connection whose monodromy is Zariski dense in $H$. Let $U$ be an irreducible analytic germ contained in a horizontal leaf, and suppose $p(U)$ is Zariski dense in $Z$. If $R$ is the Zariski closure of $U$ in $\mathcal P$, then $$\label{eq:prelim-horizontal-dimension} \dim R\geq\dim U+\dim H.$$
  - Explanation: Dense semisimple monodromy forces the connection image on the algebraic closure of a horizontal germ to fill the whole Lie algebra. Rank and kernel dimensions give the stated lower bound.
- **claim-local-orbit-filling-and-rank-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-orbit-rank)): Let $B^\circ$ be a smooth connected algebraic base carrying the marked data above, with Zariski-dense connected monodromy $G$. The same conclusions hold for their pullback by a dominant algebraic map with connected generic fiber. Consider either the joint flag map $j$, or an algebraic projective line section $\ell$ of a flat representation of $G$.* **Local orbit filling.* Let $T$ be a local fiber at a Hodge-generic point and let $Z$ be its irreducible Zariski closure. After passing to a finite level on a smooth open of $Z$, its connected monodromy is a rational normal factor $H$ of $G$. Write $G=H\times Q$. The full $Q$ period is constant on the lifted $Z$. In the flag case the $Q$ compact marks are constant there as well. At suitable smooth generic points of $T$, writing $a$ for $j$ or $\ell$, one has $$\label{eq:boundary-orbit-fill} \dim Z-\dim T=\dim(H_{\mathbb C}\cdot a),\qquad da(T_zZ)=T_{a(z)}(H_{\mathbb C}\cdot a(z)).$$ In the flag case write $J=J_H\times J_Q$ for the products of its $H$ and $Q$ flag factors, and $j=(j_H,j_Q)$ for the corresponding projections in local flat frames. The orbit in <a href="#eq:boundary-orbit-fill" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:boundary-orbit-fill">[eq:boundary-orbit-fill]</a> is then $J_H$.* **Flag ranks and lines.* For the flag map on a level of $S$, let $q:B\to S_Q$ denote a resolved connected-fiber quotient for the full $Q$ period together with its compact marks, and set $s_Q=\dim S_Q$ and $d_H=\dim J_H$. If $r=\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(dj)$, then $$\label{eq:boundary-rank-split} \mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(dj_Q)=s_Q,\qquad r=d_H+s_Q.$$ On suitable smooth projective models, with $\rho:B\to S$ the level map, the nef lines split as $$\label{eq:boundary-line-split} \rho^*\mathcal H=\mathcal H_H+\mathcal H_Q, \qquad \nu(\mathcal H_H)=d_H, \qquad \mathcal H_Q=q^*\underline{\mathcal H}_Q, \qquad \nu(\underline{\mathcal H}_Q)=s_Q.$$ Here all line equalities are rational line-bundle equalities.
  - Explanation: The algebraic closure of a local flag or projective-line fiber has rational normal-factor monodromy. Its differential fills that factor's orbit, and the complementary period is constant. For flags, this yields the corresponding rank and nef-line decomposition.
- **claim-strict-boundary-numerical-drop** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-boundary-drop)): For every component $D_i$ of $D_0$, $$\label{eq:boundary-strict-drop} \nu(\mathcal H\|_{D_i})<\nu(\mathcal H).$$ If $\nu(\mathcal H)=0$, then $D_0$ is empty.
  - Explanation: The marked nef line loses numerical dimension on every boundary component with nonidentity Lie monodromy, including finite nonidentity monodromy. A numerically trivial marked line has no such boundary.
- **claim-boundary-subtraction-section-estimate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-remove)): Let $M$ be a smooth projective $n$-fold, let $H$ be a nef rational line, and let $T=\sum_i T_i$ be a reduced divisor with smooth components. Suppose $r=\nu(H)>0$ and $\nu(H\|_{T_i})<r$ for every $i$. If $A$ is ample and $e>0$ is an integer, then $$A+tH-eT$$ is big for all sufficiently large divisible integers $t$.
  - Explanation: The ample-plus-nef section supply grows faster in the nef parameter than the losses imposed along boundary components of smaller numerical dimension. A sufficiently large divisible parameter therefore allows the stated boundary subtraction while preserving bigness.
- **claim-boundary-free-adjoint-bigness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-adjoint-bigness)): If $\dim S>0$, then $K_S+b\mathcal H$ is big for all sufficiently large rational $b$.
  - Explanation: Initial logarithmic adjoint bigness, strict boundary numerical drop, and the section estimate remove the marked boundary after increasing the nef twist.
- **claim-compatible-higgs-tail-nefness-and-rank** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-tail-rank)): Let $h:W\to S$ be any dominant morphism of smooth connected projective varieties with connected generic fiber. On a dense algebraic open of $W$, suppose that $\ell\subset h^*E_0$ is an algebraic line subbundle. Require that the pointwise instability-parabolic construction of [lem:marked-flags](#lem:marked-flags), applied to $\ell$, gives exactly the pulled-back compact marks $h^*j_{\rm u}$. This compatibility is part of the hypothesis.* *Take a smooth connected unipotent level $\pi:\widehat W\to W$ dominating the level used to define $\mathcal H$, and resolve its boundary and the generated subspace maps. Over the variation locus, let $G_i$ be the span of all contractions of $\theta^i(\ell)$ in $\pi^*h^*\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_F^{p_*-i}\mathbb A$, with $G_0=\ell$. Use the resulting subbundles of the extended Hodge grades on $\widehat W$. Put $$\label{eq:tail-line-definition} U_j=\bigoplus_{i\geq j}G_i, \qquad P=-\sum_{j\geq0}\det U_j =-\sum_{i\geq0}(i+1)\det G_i.$$ Only finitely many summands are nonzero. Then $P$ is nef and $$\label{eq:tail-rank-equality} \nu\bigl(P+\pi^*h^*\mathcal H\bigr)=\nu(P).$$ The assertion applies to every such $h$ and every such compatible line; $\ell$ need not be the original vector used to construct $S$.
  - Explanation: Determinants of the generated Higgs tails form a nef line. Null curvature directions fix the chosen projective line, its compatible compact marks, and the whole highest Hodge space, so adding the pulled-back marked line does not increase numerical dimension.
- **claim-degree-one-adjoint-annihilation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-degree-one-root)): Let $\mathfrak g=\bigoplus_k\mathfrak g_k$ be a complex semisimple Lie algebra with an integral grading and an adjoint operation $*$ carrying $\mathfrak g_k$ to $\mathfrak g_{-k}$, arising from a compatible compact form. Let $\mathfrak h$ be a graded ideal stable under $*$, and let $V$ be an irreducible finite-dimensional representation with compatible grading and highest graded piece $E$. The metric on $V$ is chosen so that representation adjoints are the actions of Lie adjoints. For $0\ne s\in E$ and $x\in\mathfrak h_1$, suppose $$\label{eq:root-annihilation-hypothesis} [x,\mathfrak h_{-1}]s=0.$$ Then $x^*E=0$.
  - Explanation: A commutator-annihilation condition on a nonzero highest graded vector forces the adjoint lowering element to annihilate the entire highest graded piece. The proof uses degree-zero group incidence, a Borel fixed point, and root-string representation theory.
- **claim-relative-iitaka-diagram-and-factorization** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-relative-iitaka-construction)): For $q:(Y_0,D_{Y_0})\to S$ and $u_*$ as above, there are smooth projective varieties $Y,W$, a birational morphism $p:Y\to Y_0$, and morphisms $x:Y\to W$ and $h:W\to S$ with geometrically connected generic fibers, such that $h\circ x=q\circ p$. Put $D=p_*^{-1}D_{Y_0}+\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\rm red}$ and $k=\kappa(Y_{0,\eta_S},K_{Y_{0,\eta_S}}+D_{Y_0,\eta_S})$. Then $k\ge0$, $\dim W-\dim S=k$, and the geometric generic fiber of $x$ has logarithmic Iitaka dimension zero. Write $J=Y_{\eta_W}$ for its generic fiber over $\mathbb C(W)$.* *The degree $a$ used for $u_*$ itself has a nonzero generator $\omega\in H^0(J,a(K_J+D_J))$. There is a rational tensor $u_W\in aK_W\otimes h^*E_0$ for which $$\label{eq:iitaka-vector-factorization} u_*=\omega\,x^*u_W.$$
  - Explanation: A scalar coordinate on the generic period fiber gives a nonzero logarithmic pluriform. The relative Iitaka construction yields a diagram with geometrically connected generic fibers and a logarithmically zero-dimensional generic source fiber. Its generator in the original homogeneous degree factors the entire vector.
- **claim-source-valuation-extraction-and-exact-sections** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-valuation-preparation)): Let $x_1:Y_1\to W_1$ be a dominant projective morphism of smooth projective varieties with geometrically connected generic fiber, and fix a reduced simple-normal-crossing divisor $D_1$ on $Y_1$. Write $J=Y_{1,\eta_{W_1}}$, and suppose that its geometric logarithmic Iitaka dimension is zero. Choose, over $\mathbb C(W_1)$, $$0\ne\omega\in H^0\bigl(J,a(K_J+D_J)\bigr)$$ for some $a>0$. Regard $\omega$ as a rational section of $aK_{Y_1/W_1}$. There is a smooth projective modification of $W_1$ with the following property. On any further smooth base model $W$, take a smooth resolution $p:Y\to Y_1$ of the main component, with $x:Y\to W$, and set $D=p_*^{-1}D_1+\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\rm red}$. For a prime $A\subset W$, put $$\label{eq:iitaka-minimum} \begin{aligned} t_A&=\min_{I\,\mapsto\,A} \frac{a^{-1}\operatorname{ord}_I(\omega)+d_I}{m_I},\\ m_I&=\operatorname{ord}_I(x^*A), \qquad d_I=\operatorname{coeff}_I D,\\ T_W&=\sum_A t_AA,\qquad L_W=K_W+T_W. \end{aligned}$$ Here $I$ runs through prime divisors dominating $A$, and the order of $\omega$ is its order as a section of $a(K_Y-x^*K_W)$. The sum defining $T_W$ is finite. For every sufficiently divisible $l>0$, multiplication gives an isomorphism $$\label{eq:iitaka-exact-sections} \begin{split} H^0(W,lL_W)&\xrightarrow{\ \simeq\ } H^0\bigl(Y_1,l(K_{Y_1}+D_1)\bigr),\\ \sigma&\longmapsto \omega^{l/a}x^*\sigma. \end{split}$$ The right side is interpreted on the fixed model $Y_1$, by rational identification. The isomorphisms respect multiplication in a common Veronese subring and preserve ratios.
  - Explanation: The preparation extracts restricted valuations of the finitely many vertical source divisors whose images have higher codimension on the temporary base. The minimum rule then tests every prime on the fixed source and identifies its complete divisible section spaces with the intermediate divisor's section spaces.
- **claim-rank-one-base-and-nef-moduli** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-rank-one-base)): Take the diagram and degree-$a$ generator $\omega$ of [lem:relative-iitaka-construction](#lem:relative-iitaka-construction). After further smooth birational preparation, with the reduced source boundary $D=p_*^{-1}D_{Y_0}+\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\rm red}$, the divisor $L_W$ defined by <a href="#eq:iitaka-minimum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:iitaka-minimum">[eq:iitaka-minimum]</a> is big over $S$. In every sufficiently divisible degree it satisfies the exact section comparison $$\label{eq:iitaka-fixed-source} H^0(W,lL_W)\simeq H^0\bigl(Y_0,l(K_{Y_0}+D_{Y_0})\bigr).$$ Moreover, the models can be chosen so that $$\label{eq:iitaka-bundle-formula} L_W=K_W+T_W=K_W+B_W+M_W, \qquad 0\le B_W\le1, \qquad M_W\ \text{nef},$$ where $B_W$ has simple-normal-crossing support and the equality uses compatible representatives of the rational divisor classes. If $I$ attains the minimum at a prime $A$, then $$\label{eq:iitaka-discriminant-bound} \operatorname{coeff}_A B_W \ge 1-\frac{1-d_I}{m_I}\ge0.$$* *After any further birational preparation of the diagram, the minimum rule can be recomputed with the higher reduced log boundary. The section comparison with the same fixed $(Y_0,D_{Y_0})$ still holds, and the moduli divisor is the trace of the same b-divisor. Once that b-divisor descends to the chosen model, all these higher traces are pullbacks of $M_W$. A further log resolution restores the simple-normal-crossing condition on $B_W$.
  - Explanation: The minimum divisor is relatively big and preserves the original base's complete divisible section spaces. An auxiliary subpair satisfies the discrepancy-sheaf rank condition and yields an effective discriminant with nef moduli part. Recomputed higher-model minima retain the same moduli b-divisor.
- **claim-base-nonvanishing-dichotomy** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-nonvanishing)): Let $f:(X,E)\to(Y,D)$ satisfy the hypotheses of [thm:upper-bound](#thm:upper-bound), and let $$0\ne s\in H^0\bigl(X,m(K_X+E)\bigr),\qquad m>0.$$ Then $\kappa(Y,L_Y)\ge0$, where $L_Y=K_Y+D$. More precisely, apply the homogeneous highest-vector and marked-period constructions to $s$, and denote their connected quotient by $S$. If $\dim S>0$, then the relative Iitaka space $W$ of [lem:relative-iitaka-construction](#lem:relative-iitaka-construction), [prop:rank-one-base](#prop:rank-one-base) satisfies $$\kappa(Y,L_Y)\ge\dim W\ge\dim S>0.$$ If $S$ is a point, there is an integer $a>0$ and a nonzero section $\tau\in H^0(Y,aL_Y)$. Moreover, for every $y\in V$ at which $s\|_{X_y}=0$, this section can be chosen with $\tau(y)=0$.
  - Explanation: A point quotient makes the coefficient system constant and gives a nonzero actual base section preserving all prescribed zeros on original smooth fibers. A positive-dimensional quotient instead forces positive base logarithmic Kodaira dimension through the intermediate space.
- **claim-period-twist-removal** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-twist-removal)): Let $h:W\to S$ be a surjective morphism of smooth projective varieties, with $\dim S>0$. Let $L$ be a rational divisor on $W$ and $H$ a nef rational divisor on $S$. Suppose:* 1. *$L$ is big on the generic fiber of $h$, and $L-h^*K_S$ is pseudoeffective;* 2. *$K_S+bH$ is big for some rational $b\geq0$;* 3. *there are a dominant generically finite morphism $\pi:\widehat W\to W$ from a smooth projective variety, a nef rational divisor $P$ on $\widehat W$, and a rational $Q>0$ such that, with $\widetilde H=\pi^*h^*H$, $$\nu(P+\widetilde H)=\nu(P),\qquad Q\pi^*L\ge_{\mathrm{pe}} P.$$* *Then $L$ is big.
  - Explanation: Relative bigness and relative pseudoeffectivity first make the period-twisted divisor big. A fixed nef comparison and numerical-dimension equality permit subtraction of that twist on a generically finite cover, giving bigness of the original divisor.
- **claim-nef-subtraction-bigness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-nef-morse)): Let $Z$ be smooth projective of dimension $n>0$, let $A$ be an ample rational divisor, and let $P,H$ be nef rational divisors satisfying $\nu(P+H)=\nu(P)$. For every fixed rational $b\ge0$, the divisor $A+tP-bH$ is big for all sufficiently large rational $t$.
  - Explanation: The numerical-dimension equality eliminates the highest-degree mixed losses in the nef Morse comparison. The ample-plus-scaled-nef supply eventually dominates the fixed nef subtraction.
- **claim-orbifold-determinant-transfer** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-orbifold-transfer)): The divisor $P_W$ is pseudoeffective and $$\label{eq:nonvanishing-pushdown} \pi^*P_W\ge_{\mathrm{pe}}P.$$ For each $i$, the determinant of the $i$th Higgs map gives a generically nonzero rational map $$\label{eq:nonvanishing-orbifold-map} \mathcal O_W(D_i^G-ar_iL_W) \longrightarrow \bigl(\Omega^1(W,B_W)\bigr)^{\otimes ir_i}.$$ This notation means the following precise assertion: after a fixed tensor power clearing the rational source divisor, the pullback map is regular on every sufficiently divisible adapted cover, using the orbifold cotangent lattice in codimension one. The same tensor power works after replacing $B_W$ by any effective SNC boundary $B_W+\Lambda$ with coefficients at most one.
  - Explanation: The descended tail divisor is pseudoeffective and dominates the nef tail after pullback. Using the same minimizing source divisor for both the section lattice and discriminant estimate makes determinant Higgs maps regular in the adapted orbifold cotangent lattice.
- **claim-uniform-pseudoeffective-domination** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pseff-threshold)): In the notation above, $L_W$ is pseudoeffective. There is a constant $Q>0$, depending only on the fixed Higgs maps, such that $$\label{eq:nonvanishing-domination} Q\pi^*L_W\ge_{\mathrm{pe}}P.$$
  - Explanation: Fixed determinant maps and orbifold cotangent positivity yield movable-curve inequalities independent of the ample perturbation. The threshold argument proves pseudoeffectivity and a fixed positive multiple dominating the nef tail on the cover.
- **claim-relative-pseudoeffectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-nonvanishing-relative)): For the prepared morphism $h:W\to S$ one has $$\label{eq:nonvanishing-relative} L_W-h^*K_S\ge_{\mathrm{pe}}0.$$
  - Explanation: The prepared fibration and ample boundary perturbations permit application of relative orbifold positivity on suitable birational models. Effective discrepancy terms and pushforward give relative pseudoeffectivity on the fixed intermediate base after the perturbation tends to zero.
- **claim-positive-period-intermediate-bigness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-positive-period)): For the data attached to a nonzero total-space pluriform, if $\dim S>0$ then $L_W$ is big and $$\kappa(Y,L_Y)\ge\dim W>0.$$
  - Explanation: The intermediate divisor satisfies every hypothesis of period-twist removal and is therefore big. Its exact divisible section lattice returns enough actual base sections to force positive base logarithmic Kodaira dimension.
- **claim-companion-logarithmic-subadditivity** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-log-iitaka)): Let $g\colon Z\to T$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibers between smooth connected projective complex varieties. Let $B_Z,B_T$ be reduced SNC divisors with $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}(g^*B_T)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}(B_Z)$, and let $G$ be a very general smooth fiber with $B_G=B_Z\|_G$. Then $$\label{eq:lower-bound} \kappa(Z,K_Z+B_Z) \geq \kappa(T,K_T+B_T)+\kappa(G,K_G+B_G).$$ No smoothness outside $B_T$, abundance, or good-minimal-model hypothesis is imposed.
  - Explanation: The companion theorem supplies the lower logarithmic Kodaira inequality for connected-fiber projective morphisms of reduced SNC pairs satisfying support inclusion. It requires neither stratum smoothness nor minimal-model assumptions and is used here only to obtain additivity.

        "exact_quote": "Let $f,E,D$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:upper-bound\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:upper-bound\">1</a>, and let $0\\ne s\\in H^0(X,mL_X)$ for some $m>0$.         "exact_quote": "There are a dense smooth open $Y^\\circ\\subset V$, a polarizable pure rational variation $\\mathbb V$ on $Y^\\circ$ with an invariant lattice and quasi-unipotent boundary monodromy, and a nonzero algebraic map $$\\label{eq:log-input-vector}\n u:\\mathcal O_{Y^\\circ}(-L_Y)\\longrightarrow\n \\mathcal E^d,\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal E^p=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}_F^p(\\mathbb V\\otimes\\mathcal O_{Y^\\circ}),\n \\qquad F^{d+1}\\mathbb V=0.$$ On every model $(Y',D')$ just described, the rationally identified map and all its Higgs iterates $$\\label{eq:log-input-iterates}\n \\theta^j(u):\\mathcal O_{Y'}(-L_{Y'})\\dashrightarrow\n \\mathcal E^{d-j}\\otimes(B')^{\\otimes j}$$ have regular coefficients at every prime of $Y'$ in the preceding sense. Here the variation and its Hodge bundles are understood over the common dense open, and a grade outside their range is zero.*\n\n\n*The same statement holds for the homogeneous tensor replacement used in [prop:marked-period](#prop:marked-period). More precisely, if $u_*$ is obtained from $u$ by homogeneous tensor operations of positive degree $a$ and flat Hodge-compatible projections, then $$\\label{eq:log-input-tensor}\n u_*:\\mathcal O(-aL_{Y'})\\dashrightarrow E_0,\n \\qquad\n \\theta^j(u_*):\\mathcal O(-aL_{Y'})\\dashrightarrow\n E_j\\otimes(B')^{\\otimes j}$$ have the same coefficient property. Here $\\mathbb A$ is the resulting pure variation, $p_*$ is its highest nonzero Hodge index, and $E_j=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}_F^{p_*-j}\\mathbb A$. Finite levels and a common further unipotent ramification may be used in these assertions.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-weight-extension",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $g:Z\\to\\Delta$ is projective and has the local form <a href=\"#eq:log-input-semistable-coordinates\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:log-input-semistable-coordinates\">[eq:log-input-semistable-coordinates]</a>, with $Z$ smooth, reduced special fiber $V_0$, and horizontal boundary $H$. For fixed $i,j$, the relative log-form cohomology $$\\mathcal A^{i,j}=R^j g_*\\Omega^i_{Z/\\Delta}(\\log(V_0+H))$$ is locally free and carries a filtration by subbundles extending the weight filtration on its Hodge grade over $\\Delta^*$. Its successive quotients are the Hodge-graded unipotent canonical extensions of the corresponding pure weight variations. In particular, a regular class that belongs generically to a weight step projects regularly to that step’s pure weight quotient.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-zero-test",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\dim Y>0$ and $y\\in V$, and suppose the section $s$ in <a href=\"#eq:log-input-section\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:log-input-section\">[eq:log-input-section]</a> vanishes identically on $f^{-1}(y)$. Blow up $y$ on the base if $\\dim Y>1$, and let $A$ be its exceptional divisor; if $\\dim Y=1$, put $A=\\{y\\}$. Along a general transverse test at $A$, after ramification of order $e$ divisible by $m$, the coefficients of $u$ in the pulled-back *original* $\\mathcal O_Y(-L_Y)$ frame vanish to order at least $e/m$. Every homogeneous replacement $u_*$ of degree $a>0$ vanishes to order at least $ae/m$ in the corresponding original $\\mathcal O_Y(-aL_Y)$ frame.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-marked-period",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B^\\circ$ be a smooth irreducible complex quasi-projective variety, and let $\\mathbb V$ be a polarizable rational pure variation with an invariant integral lattice. Suppose that a line bundle $\\mathcal L$ has a generically nonzero algebraic morphism $$u:\\mathcal L\\longrightarrow F^p\\mathbb V_{\\mathbb C},\n \\qquad F^{p+1}\\mathbb V_{\\mathbb C}=0.$$ There are a positive integer $a$, a homogeneous tensor construction followed by flat Hodge-compatible projections, and a generically nonzero morphism $$\\label{eq:marked-vector}\n u_*:\\mathcal L^{\\otimes a}\\longrightarrow E_0,\n \\qquad E_0=F^{p_*}\\mathbb A,\n \\qquad F^{p_*+1}\\mathbb A=0,$$ where $\\mathbb A$ is a polarized complex pure variation. The operations may first be performed equivariantly at finite monodromy level. They descend to $B^\\circ$ after shrinking it. On smooth projective birational models there is a dominant morphism $b:B\\to S$ with connected fibers and the following properties.*\n\n1.  **Descent.* On dense smooth opens, $\\mathbb A$ is the pullback of an actual variation on $S^\\circ$. Its connected representation is irreducible and factors through a rational semisimple adjoint group $G$. The full monodromy acts through its projected action on $\\mathfrak g=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}G$. In particular, finite scalar monodromy in the kernel of that action has been eliminated.*\n\n2.  **The marks.* There is an algebraic compact-flag section $j_{\\mathrm u}$, possibly empty, determined pointwise and equivariantly by $[u_*]$. In flat coordinates the joint marks $$j=(E_0,j_{\\mathrm u})$$ take values in a closed product flag orbit $J$ of $G_{\\mathbb C}$. The two kinds of marks use disjoint complex simple factors. Every rational simple factor of $G$ has a complex factor acting nontrivially on $J$. The full Lie-period map together with $j_{\\mathrm u}$ is generically immersive on $S^\\circ$.*\n\n\n\n3.  **The nef lines and adjoint positivity.* If $S$ is a point, $\\mathbb A$ is constant and we set $\\mathcal H=\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}=0$. Otherwise $S$ has nef rational line bundles $\\mathcal H$ and $\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}$, with $\\mathcal H-\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}$ nef. On a smooth unipotent connected-monodromy cover their pullbacks are respectively $$\\det E_0+\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}\n     \\quad\\hbox{and the positive compact-flag line.}$$ The generic curvature rank of $\\mathcal H$ is the generic rank of $dj$, and equals $\\nu(\\mathcal H)$. If $D_0$ denotes the reduced union of boundary components with nonidentity local monodromy on $\\mathfrak g$, then $$\\label{eq:marked-initial-big}\n     K_S+D_0+c\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}\\quad\\text{is big for }c\\gg0.$$ Furthermore, $$\\label{eq:marked-final-big}\n     K_S+b_0\\mathcal H\\quad\\text{is big for }b_0\\gg0.$$*\n\n*All assertions about extensions are compatible with further smooth projective modifications and pullback at unipotent level. In particular, the iterated Higgs fields of $u_*$ are polynomial tensor expressions in those of $u$; the construction introduces no division by a local function.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-marked-period",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B^\\circ$ be a smooth irreducible complex quasi-projective variety, and let $\\mathbb V$ be a polarizable rational pure variation with an invariant integral lattice. The operations may first be performed equivariantly at finite monodromy level. They descend to $B^\\circ$ after shrinking it. On smooth projective birational models there is a dominant morphism $b:B\\to S$ with connected fibers and the following properties.*\n\n1.  **Descent.* On dense smooth opens, $\\mathbb A$ is the pullback of an actual variation on $S^\\circ$. Its connected representation is irreducible and factors through a rational semisimple adjoint group $G$. The full monodromy acts through its projected action on $\\mathfrak g=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}G$. In particular, finite scalar monodromy in the kernel of that action has been eliminated.*\n\n2.  **The marks.* There is an algebraic compact-flag section $j_{\\mathrm u}$, possibly empty, determined pointwise and equivariantly by $[u_*]$. In flat coordinates the joint marks $$j=(E_0,j_{\\mathrm u})$$ take values in a closed product flag orbit $J$ of $G_{\\mathbb C}$. The two kinds of marks use disjoint complex simple factors. Every rational simple factor of $G$ has a complex factor acting nontrivially on $J$. The full Lie-period map together with $j_{\\mathrm u}$ is generically immersive on $S^\\circ$.*\n\n\n\n3.  **The nef lines and adjoint positivity.* If $S$ is a point, $\\mathbb A$ is constant and we set $\\mathcal H=\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}=0$. Otherwise $S$ has nef rational line bundles $\\mathcal H$ and $\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}$, with $\\mathcal H-\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}$ nef. On a smooth unipotent connected-monodromy cover their pullbacks are respectively $$\\det E_0+\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}\n     \\quad\\hbox{and the positive compact-flag line.}$$ The generic curvature rank of $\\mathcal H$ is the generic rank of $dj$, and equals $\\nu(\\mathcal H)$. If $D_0$ denotes the reduced union of boundary components with nonidentity local monodromy on $\\mathfrak g$, then $$\\label{eq:marked-initial-big}\n     K_S+D_0+c\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}\\quad\\text{is big for }c\\gg0.$$ Furthermore, $$\\label{eq:marked-final-big}\n     K_S+b_0\\mathcal H\\quad\\text{is big for }b_0\\gg0.$$*\n\n*All assertions about extensions are compatible with further smooth projective modifications and pullback at unipotent level. In particular, the iterated Higgs fields of $u_*$ are polynomial tensor expressions in those of $u$; the construction introduces no division by a local function.",

      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $B^\\circ$ be a smooth irreducible complex quasi-projective variety, and let $\\mathbb V$ be a polarizable rational pure variation with an invariant integral lattice. Suppose that a line bundle $\\mathcal L$ has a generically nonzero algebraic morphism $$u:\\mathcal L\\longrightarrow F^p\\mathbb V_{\\mathbb C},\n \\qquad F^{p+1}\\mathbb V_{\\mathbb C}=0.$$ There are a positive integer $a$, a homogeneous tensor construction followed by flat Hodge-compatible projections, and a generically nonzero morphism $$\\label{eq:marked-vector}\n u_*:\\mathcal L^{\\otimes a}\\longrightarrow E_0,\n \\qquad E_0=F^{p_*}\\mathbb A,\n \\qquad F^{p_*+1}\\mathbb A=0,$$ where $\\mathbb A$ is a polarized complex pure variation. The operations may first be performed equivariantly at finite monodromy level. They descend to $B^\\circ$ after shrinking it. On smooth projective birational models there is a dominant morphism $b:B\\to S$ with connected fibers and the following properties.*\n\n1.  **Descent.* On dense smooth opens, $\\mathbb A$ is the pullback of an actual variation on $S^\\circ$. Its connected representation is irreducible and factors through a rational semisimple adjoint group $G$. The full monodromy acts through its projected action on $\\mathfrak g=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}G$. In particular, finite scalar monodromy in the kernel of that action has been eliminated.*\n\n2.  **The marks.* There is an algebraic compact-flag section $j_{\\mathrm u}$, possibly empty, determined pointwise and equivariantly by $[u_*]$. In flat coordinates the joint marks $$j=(E_0,j_{\\mathrm u})$$ take values in a closed product flag orbit $J$ of $G_{\\mathbb C}$. The two kinds of marks use disjoint complex simple factors. Every rational simple factor of $G$ has a complex factor acting nontrivially on $J$. The full Lie-period map together with $j_{\\mathrm u}$ is generically immersive on $S^\\circ$.*\n\n\n\n3.  **The nef lines and adjoint positivity.* If $S$ is a point, $\\mathbb A$ is constant and we set $\\mathcal H=\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}=0$. Otherwise $S$ has nef rational line bundles $\\mathcal H$ and $\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}$, with $\\mathcal H-\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}$ nef. On a smooth unipotent connected-monodromy cover their pullbacks are respectively $$\\det E_0+\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}\n     \\quad\\hbox{and the positive compact-flag line.}$$ The generic curvature rank of $\\mathcal H$ is the generic rank of $dj$, and equals $\\nu(\\mathcal H)$. If $D_0$ denotes the reduced union of boundary components with nonidentity local monodromy on $\\mathfrak g$, then $$\\label{eq:marked-initial-big}\n     K_S+D_0+c\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}\\quad\\text{is big for }c\\gg0.$$ Furthermore, $$\\label{eq:marked-final-big}\n     K_S+b_0\\mathcal H\\quad\\text{is big for }b_0\\gg0.$$*\n\n*All assertions about extensions are compatible with further smooth projective modifications and pullback at unipotent level. In particular, the iterated Higgs fields of $u_*$ are polynomial tensor expressions in those of $u$; the construction introduces no division by a local function.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-marked-monodromy",
        "exact_quote": "The group $G_c$ is semisimple. At connected monodromy level the Hodge circles normalize $G_c$, are locally conjugate by $G_c(\\mathbb R)^+$, and have locally constant action on every $G_c$-invariant tensor space. The horizontal period differential therefore has the form $$\\label{eq:marked-period-tangent}\n t(v)\\in\\mathfrak g_{c,\\mathbb C}^{-1,1},\n \\qquad \\theta(v)=d\\rho\\bigl(t(v)\\bigr),\n \\qquad [t(v),t(w)]=0.$$ The Lie algebra, and every monodromy-stable sum of its rational simple ideals, is a polarized rational integral variation of weight zero.         "source_id": "PM-033.3"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-marked-replacement",
        "exact_quote": "The replacement <a href=\"#eq:marked-vector\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:marked-vector\">[eq:marked-vector]</a> can be chosen so that its connected representation is irreducible and its full representation factors through the action on the retained rational adjoint Lie factors. Among all such homogeneous replacements, choose one with the fewest retained rational simple factors. Write $G$ for their product as an adjoint group and $\\mathfrak g=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}G$.*\n\n*Every representation factor used in splitting $\\mathbb A$ according to a product decomposition of $G$ is a Hodge-compatible summand of tensor constructions on the corresponding Lie variation, up to a constant shift of the Hodge indices.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-log-metric",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a smooth projective variety of dimension $n$, let $\\Delta$ be an SNC divisor, and let $L$ be a rational line bundle. Suppose that on $Z^\\circ=Z\\setminus\\Delta$ it has a smooth Hermitian metric with semipositive curvature form $\\Theta$, normalized to represent $c_1(L)$. Suppose that in regular nonvanishing local frames of a Cartier multiple of $L$ the metric weights satisfy $$\\label{eq:prelim-loglog-bound}\n |\\varphi(z)|\\leq C\\left(1+\n       \\sum_{i=1}^{\\ell}\\log(-\\log|z_i|)\\right)$$ near every boundary chart $\\Delta=\\{z_1\\cdots z_\\ell=0\\}$. Then $L$ is nef. For every Kähler form $\\eta$ on $Z$ and $0\\leq k\\leq n$, $$\\label{eq:prelim-mass-identity}\n \\int_{Z^\\circ}\\Theta^k\\wedge\\eta^{n-k}\n       =c_1(L)^k[\\eta]^{n-k}.$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:prelim-curvature-rank}\n \\nu(L)=\\max_{z\\in Z^\\circ}\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}\\Theta_z.$$ When the curvature is real analytic, as for the induced Hodge and subspace metrics used below, this maximum is its generic rank.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-connection-dimension",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p:\\mathcal P\\to Z$ be an algebraic principal bundle for a connected semisimple complex algebraic group $H$, where $Z$ is smooth and irreducible. Let $U$ be an irreducible analytic germ contained in a horizontal leaf, and suppose $p(U)$ is Zariski dense in $Z$. If $R$ is the Zariski closure of $U$ in $\\mathcal P$, then $$\\label{eq:prelim-horizontal-dimension}\n \\dim R\\geq\\dim U+\\dim H.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-033.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-connection-dimension",
          "exact_quote": "Let $p:\\mathcal P\\to Z$ be an algebraic principal bundle for a connected semisimple complex algebraic group $H$, where $Z$ is smooth and irreducible. Suppose $\\mathcal P$ carries an algebraic flat principal connection whose monodromy is Zariski dense in $H$. Let $U$ be an irreducible analytic germ contained in a horizontal leaf, and suppose $p(U)$ is Zariski dense in $Z$. If $R$ is the Zariski closure of $U$ in $\\mathcal P$, then $$\\label{eq:prelim-horizontal-dimension}\n \\dim R\\geq\\dim U+\\dim H.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-orbit-rank",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B^\\circ$ be a smooth connected algebraic base carrying the marked data above, with Zariski-dense connected monodromy $G$. The same conclusions hold for their pullback by a dominant algebraic map with connected generic fiber. Consider either the joint flag map $j$, or an algebraic projective line section $\\ell$ of a flat representation of $G$.*\n\n\n**Local orbit filling.* Let $T$ be a local fiber at a Hodge-generic point and let $Z$ be its irreducible Zariski closure. After passing to a finite level on a smooth open of $Z$, its connected monodromy is a rational normal factor $H$ of $G$. Write $G=H\\times Q$. The full $Q$ period is constant on the lifted $Z$. In the flag case the $Q$ compact marks are constant there as well. At suitable smooth generic points of $T$, writing $a$ for $j$ or $\\ell$, one has $$\\label{eq:boundary-orbit-fill}\n \\dim Z-\\dim T=\\dim(H_{\\mathbb C}\\cdot a),\\qquad\n da(T_zZ)=T_{a(z)}(H_{\\mathbb C}\\cdot a(z)).$$ In the flag case write $J=J_H\\times J_Q$ for the products of its $H$ and $Q$ flag factors, and $j=(j_H,j_Q)$ for the corresponding projections in local flat frames. The orbit in <a href=\"#eq:boundary-orbit-fill\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:boundary-orbit-fill\">[eq:boundary-orbit-fill]</a> is then $J_H$.*\n\n\n\n**Flag ranks and lines.* For the flag map on a level of $S$, let $q:B\\to S_Q$ denote a resolved connected-fiber quotient for the full $Q$ period together with its compact marks, and set $s_Q=\\dim S_Q$ and $d_H=\\dim J_H$. If $r=\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}(dj)$, then $$\\label{eq:boundary-rank-split}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}(dj_Q)=s_Q,\\qquad r=d_H+s_Q.$$ On suitable smooth projective models, with $\\rho:B\\to S$ the level map, the nef lines split as $$\\label{eq:boundary-line-split}\n \\rho^*\\mathcal H=\\mathcal H_H+\\mathcal H_Q,\n \\qquad \\nu(\\mathcal H_H)=d_H,\n \\qquad \\mathcal H_Q=q^*\\underline{\\mathcal H}_Q,\n \\qquad \\nu(\\underline{\\mathcal H}_Q)=s_Q.$$ Here all line equalities are rational line-bundle equalities.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.3"
      },

If $r=\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}(dj)$, then $$\\label{eq:boundary-rank-split}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}(dj_Q)=s_Q,\\qquad r=d_H+s_Q.$$ On suitable smooth projective models, with $\\rho:B\\to S$ the level map, the nef lines split as $$\\label{eq:boundary-line-split}\n \\rho^*\\mathcal H=\\mathcal H_H+\\mathcal H_Q,\n \\qquad \\nu(\\mathcal H_H)=d_H,\n \\qquad \\mathcal H_Q=q^*\\underline{\\mathcal H}_Q,\n \\qquad \\nu(\\underline{\\mathcal H}_Q)=s_Q.$$ Here all line equalities are rational line-bundle equalities.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-remove",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a smooth projective $n$-fold, let $H$ be a nef rational line, and let $T=\\sum_i T_i$ be a reduced divisor with smooth components. Suppose $r=\\nu(H)>0$ and $\\nu(H|_{T_i})<r$ for every $i$. If $A$ is ample and $e>0$ is an integer, then $$A+tH-eT$$ is big for all sufficiently large divisible integers $t$.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-remove",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a smooth projective $n$-fold, let $H$ be a nef rational line, and let $T=\\sum_i T_i$ be a reduced divisor with smooth components.         }

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-tail-rank",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h:W\\to S$ be any dominant morphism of smooth connected projective varieties with connected generic fiber. On a dense algebraic open of $W$, suppose that $\\ell\\subset h^*E_0$ is an algebraic line subbundle. Require that the pointwise instability-parabolic construction of [lem:marked-flags](#lem:marked-flags), applied to $\\ell$, gives exactly the pulled-back compact marks $h^*j_{\\rm u}$. This compatibility is part of the hypothesis.*\n\n\n*Take a smooth connected unipotent level $\\pi:\\widehat W\\to W$ dominating the level used to define $\\mathcal H$, and resolve its boundary and the generated subspace maps. Over the variation locus, let $G_i$ be the span of all contractions of $\\theta^i(\\ell)$ in $\\pi^*h^*\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}_F^{p_*-i}\\mathbb A$, with $G_0=\\ell$. Use the resulting subbundles of the extended Hodge grades on $\\widehat W$. Put $$\\label{eq:tail-line-definition}\n U_j=\\bigoplus_{i\\geq j}G_i,\n \\qquad\n P=-\\sum_{j\\geq0}\\det U_j\n   =-\\sum_{i\\geq0}(i+1)\\det G_i.$$ Only finitely many summands are nonzero. Then $P$ is nef and $$\\label{eq:tail-rank-equality}\n              \\nu\\bigl(P+\\pi^*h^*\\mathcal H\\bigr)=\\nu(P).$$ The assertion applies to every such $h$ and every such compatible line; $\\ell$ need not be the original vector used to construct $S$.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-tail-rank",
          "exact_quote": "Let $h:W\\to S$ be any dominant morphism of smooth connected projective varieties with connected generic fiber. Over the variation locus, let $G_i$ be the span of all contractions of $\\theta^i(\\ell)$ in $\\pi^*h^*\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}_F^{p_*-i}\\mathbb A$, with $G_0=\\ell$. Use the resulting subbundles of the extended Hodge grades on $\\widehat W$. Put $$\\label{eq:tail-line-definition}\n U_j=\\bigoplus_{i\\geq j}G_i,\n \\qquad\n P=-\\sum_{j\\geq0}\\det U_j\n   =-\\sum_{i\\geq0}(i+1)\\det G_i.$$ Only finitely many summands are nonzero. Then $P$ is nef and $$\\label{eq:tail-rank-equality}\n              \\nu\\bigl(P+\\pi^*h^*\\mathcal H\\bigr)=\\nu(P).$$ The assertion applies to every such $h$ and every such compatible line; $\\ell$ need not be the original vector used to construct $S$.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.3"

      "exact_statement": "Let $h:W\\to S$ be any dominant morphism of smooth connected projective varieties with connected generic fiber.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-degree-one-root",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathfrak g=\\bigoplus_k\\mathfrak g_k$ be a complex semisimple Lie algebra with an integral grading and an adjoint operation $*$ carrying $\\mathfrak g_k$ to $\\mathfrak g_{-k}$, arising from a compatible compact form. Let $\\mathfrak h$ be a graded ideal stable under $*$, and let $V$ be an irreducible finite-dimensional representation with compatible grading and highest graded piece $E$. The metric on $V$ is chosen so that representation adjoints are the actions of Lie adjoints. For $0\\ne s\\in E$ and $x\\in\\mathfrak h_1$, suppose $$\\label{eq:root-annihilation-hypothesis}\n                         [x,\\mathfrak h_{-1}]s=0.$$ Then $x^*E=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.3"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-relative-iitaka-construction",
        "exact_quote": "For $q:(Y_0,D_{Y_0})\\to S$ and $u_*$ as above, there are smooth projective varieties $Y,W$, a birational morphism $p:Y\\to Y_0$, and morphisms $x:Y\\to W$ and $h:W\\to S$ with geometrically connected generic fibers, such that $h\\circ x=q\\circ p$. Put $D=p_*^{-1}D_{Y_0}+\\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\\rm red}$ and $k=\\kappa(Y_{0,\\eta_S},K_{Y_{0,\\eta_S}}+D_{Y_0,\\eta_S})$. Then $k\\ge0$, $\\dim W-\\dim S=k$, and the geometric generic fiber of $x$ has logarithmic Iitaka dimension zero. Write $J=Y_{\\eta_W}$ for its generic fiber over $\\mathbb C(W)$.*\n\n\n*The degree $a$ used for $u_*$ itself has a nonzero generator $\\omega\\in H^0(J,a(K_J+D_J))$. There is a rational tensor $u_W\\in aK_W\\otimes h^*E_0$ for which $$\\label{eq:iitaka-vector-factorization}\n u_*=\\omega\\,x^*u_W.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-valuation-preparation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $x_1:Y_1\\to W_1$ be a dominant projective morphism of smooth projective varieties with geometrically connected generic fiber, and fix a reduced simple-normal-crossing divisor $D_1$ on $Y_1$. Write $J=Y_{1,\\eta_{W_1}}$, and suppose that its geometric logarithmic Iitaka dimension is zero. Choose, over $\\mathbb C(W_1)$, $$0\\ne\\omega\\in H^0\\bigl(J,a(K_J+D_J)\\bigr)$$ for some $a>0$. Regard $\\omega$ as a rational section of $aK_{Y_1/W_1}$. There is a smooth projective modification of $W_1$ with the following property. On any further smooth base model $W$, take a smooth resolution $p:Y\\to Y_1$ of the main component, with $x:Y\\to W$, and set $D=p_*^{-1}D_1+\\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\\rm red}$. For a prime $A\\subset W$, put $$\\label{eq:iitaka-minimum}\n \\begin{aligned}\n t_A&=\\min_{I\\,\\mapsto\\,A}\n       \\frac{a^{-1}\\operatorname{ord}_I(\\omega)+d_I}{m_I},\\\\\n m_I&=\\operatorname{ord}_I(x^*A),\n       \\qquad d_I=\\operatorname{coeff}_I D,\\\\\n T_W&=\\sum_A t_AA,\\qquad L_W=K_W+T_W.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Here $I$ runs through prime divisors dominating $A$, and the order of $\\omega$ is its order as a section of $a(K_Y-x^*K_W)$. The sum defining $T_W$ is finite. For every sufficiently divisible $l>0$, multiplication gives an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:iitaka-exact-sections}\n \\begin{split}\n H^0(W,lL_W)&\\xrightarrow{\\ \\simeq\\ }\n       H^0\\bigl(Y_1,l(K_{Y_1}+D_1)\\bigr),\\\\\n \\sigma&\\longmapsto \\omega^{l/a}x^*\\sigma.\n \\end{split}$$ The right side is interpreted on the fixed model $Y_1$, by rational identification. The isomorphisms respect multiplication in a common Veronese subring and preserve ratios.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-rank-one-base",
        "exact_quote": "Take the diagram and degree-$a$ generator $\\omega$ of [lem:relative-iitaka-construction](#lem:relative-iitaka-construction). After further smooth birational preparation, with the reduced source boundary $D=p_*^{-1}D_{Y_0}+\\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\\rm red}$, the divisor $L_W$ defined by <a href=\"#eq:iitaka-minimum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:iitaka-minimum\">[eq:iitaka-minimum]</a> is big over $S$. In every sufficiently divisible degree it satisfies the exact section comparison $$\\label{eq:iitaka-fixed-source}\n H^0(W,lL_W)\\simeq\n H^0\\bigl(Y_0,l(K_{Y_0}+D_{Y_0})\\bigr).$$ Moreover, the models can be chosen so that $$\\label{eq:iitaka-bundle-formula}\n L_W=K_W+T_W=K_W+B_W+M_W,\n \\qquad 0\\le B_W\\le1,\n \\qquad M_W\\ \\text{nef},$$ where $B_W$ has simple-normal-crossing support and the equality uses compatible representatives of the rational divisor classes. If $I$ attains the minimum at a prime $A$, then $$\\label{eq:iitaka-discriminant-bound}\n \\operatorname{coeff}_A B_W\n       \\ge 1-\\frac{1-d_I}{m_I}\\ge0.$$*\n\n*After any further birational preparation of the diagram, the minimum rule can be recomputed with the higher reduced log boundary. The section comparison with the same fixed $(Y_0,D_{Y_0})$ still holds, and the moduli divisor is the trace of the same b-divisor. Once that b-divisor descends to the chosen model, all these higher traces are pullbacks of $M_W$. A further log resolution restores the simple-normal-crossing condition on $B_W$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-nonvanishing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:(X,E)\\to(Y,D)$ satisfy the hypotheses of [thm:upper-bound](#thm:upper-bound), and let $$0\\ne s\\in H^0\\bigl(X,m(K_X+E)\\bigr),\\qquad m>0.$$ Then $\\kappa(Y,L_Y)\\ge0$, where $L_Y=K_Y+D$. More precisely, apply the homogeneous highest-vector and marked-period constructions to $s$, and denote their connected quotient by $S$. If $\\dim S>0$, then the relative Iitaka space $W$ of [lem:relative-iitaka-construction](#lem:relative-iitaka-construction), [prop:rank-one-base](#prop:rank-one-base) satisfies $$\\kappa(Y,L_Y)\\ge\\dim W\\ge\\dim S>0.$$ If $S$ is a point, there is an integer $a>0$ and a nonzero section $\\tau\\in H^0(Y,aL_Y)$. Moreover, for every $y\\in V$ at which $s|_{X_y}=0$, this section can be chosen with $\\tau(y)=0$.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-twist-removal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h:W\\to S$ be a surjective morphism of smooth projective varieties, with $\\dim S>0$. Let $L$ be a rational divisor on $W$ and $H$ a nef rational divisor on $S$. Suppose:*\n\n1.  *$L$ is big on the generic fiber of $h$, and $L-h^*K_S$ is pseudoeffective;*\n\n2.  *$K_S+bH$ is big for some rational $b\\geq0$;*\n\n3.  *there are a dominant generically finite morphism $\\pi:\\widehat W\\to W$ from a smooth projective variety, a nef rational divisor $P$ on $\\widehat W$, and a rational $Q>0$ such that, with $\\widetilde H=\\pi^*h^*H$, $$\\nu(P+\\widetilde H)=\\nu(P),\\qquad Q\\pi^*L\\ge_{\\mathrm{pe}} P.$$*\n\n*Then $L$ is big.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-twist-removal",
          "exact_quote": "Let $h:W\\to S$ be a surjective morphism of smooth projective varieties, with $\\dim S>0$. Let $L$ be a rational divisor on $W$ and $H$ a nef rational divisor on $S$. Suppose:*\n\n1.  *$L$ is big on the generic fiber of $h$, and $L-h^*K_S$ is pseudoeffective;*\n\n2.  *$K_S+bH$ is big for some rational $b\\geq0$;*\n\n3.  *there are a dominant generically finite morphism $\\pi:\\widehat W\\to W$ from a smooth projective variety, a nef rational divisor $P$ on $\\widehat W$, and a rational $Q>0$ such that, with $\\widetilde H=\\pi^*h^*H$, $$\\nu(P+\\widetilde H)=\\nu(P),\\qquad Q\\pi^*L\\ge_{\\mathrm{pe}} P.$$*\n\n*Then $L$ is big.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.3"
        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Logarithmic canonical divisors** ([source](#eq:support-inclusion)): Write $$L_X=K_X+E,\qquad L_Y=K_Y+D,\qquad L_F=K_F+E_F.$$
- **Original open base and fiber pair** ([source](#eq:support-inclusion)): Set $V=Y\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}D$.
- **Stratum smoothness** ([source](#eq:support-inclusion)): We assume that $X$ and every irreducible component of every nonempty intersection of components of $E$, restricted to $f^{-1}(V)$, are smooth over $V$. We call this *stratum smoothness over $V$*.
- **Extended Kodaira dimension conventions** ([source](#eq:support-inclusion)): We give a point Kodaira dimension zero and use $(-\infty)+a=-\infty$, also when $a=-\infty$.
- **Pseudoeffective comparison** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For numerical divisor classes, $P\ge_{\mathrm{pe}}Q$ means that $P-Q$ is pseudoeffective.
- **Higher reduced logarithmic boundary** ([source](#pm-h-8)): If $\mu:Z'\to Z$ is a birational morphism between smooth projective varieties and $\Delta$ is reduced SNC, put $$\Delta'=\mu^{-1}_*\Delta+\operatorname{Exc}(\mu)_{\mathrm{red}},$$ after further resolution when necessary.
- **Numerical dimension of a nef divisor** ([source](#eq:prelim-numerical-dimension)): If $P$ is nef on an $n$-dimensional projective variety, its numerical dimension is $$\label{eq:prelim-numerical-dimension} \nu(P)=\max\{k\in\{0,\ldots,n\}:P^k A^{n-k}>0\}, \qquad A\ \text{ample},$$ independently of $A$, and $P$ is big exactly when $\nu(P)=n$.
- **Polarized rational pure variation** ([source](#pm-h-13)): A polarized rational variation of pure Hodge structure of weight $w$ on a smooth quasi-projective variety $B^\circ$ consists of a rational local system $\mathbb V_{\mathbb Q}$, its flat holomorphic bundle $\mathcal V=\mathbb V_{\mathbb Q}\otimes_{\mathbb Q}\mathcal O_{B^\circ}$ with connection $\nabla$, a decreasing holomorphic filtration $F^\bullet\mathcal V$, and a flat polarization.
- **Unipotent canonical extension** ([source](#pm-h-13)): At unipotent level, let $\overline{\mathcal V}$ denote Deligne’s canonical extension, with nilpotent logarithmic residues.
- **Regular coefficients in pulled-back logarithmic frames** ([source](#pm-h-14)): A tensor has *regular coefficients* if, in the Schmid extension of its Hodge factor, its coefficients relative to the *pulled-back chosen frames* are regular.
- **Connected monodromy level** ([source](#pm-h-16)): A connected monodromy level means a connected finite étale cover on which the monodromy lies in $G_c$; its image is still Zariski dense there.
- **Invisible retained rational factor** ([source](#pm-h-17)): Call a retained rational simple factor *invisible* if all its complex factors act trivially on the orbit of $E_0$.
- **Marked nonidentity boundary** ([source](#pm-h-24)): The reduced divisor $D_0$ on $S$ consists of the boundary components whose monodromy on the retained rational Lie variation $\mathfrak g$ is not the identity.
- **Generated Higgs tails and weighted determinant line** ([source](#stmt-prop-tail-rank)): Put $$\label{eq:tail-line-definition} U_j=\bigoplus_{i\geq j}G_i, \qquad P=-\sum_{j\geq0}\det U_j =-\sum_{i\geq0}(i+1)\det G_i.$$
- **Minimum section-lattice divisor** ([source](#stmt-lem-valuation-preparation)): For a prime $A\subset W$, put $$\label{eq:iitaka-minimum} \begin{aligned} t_A&=\min_{I\,\mapsto\,A} \frac{a^{-1}\operatorname{ord}_I(\omega)+d_I}{m_I},\\ m_I&=\operatorname{ord}_I(x^*A), \qquad d_I=\operatorname{coeff}_I D,\\ T_W&=\sum_A t_AA,\qquad L_W=K_W+T_W. \end{aligned}$$
- **Auxiliary lc-trivial subpair** ([source](#eq:iitaka-auxiliary-pair)): On the current smooth diagram define $$\label{eq:iitaka-auxiliary-pair} \Delta=-\frac1a\operatorname{div}_{aK_{Y/W}}(\omega)+x^*T_W.$$
- **Fixed descended determinant divisors** ([source](#pm-h-33)): Define rational divisors on $W$ by $$\label{eq:nonvanishing-determinants} D_i^G=\frac{\pi_*\widehat D_i^G}{\deg\pi},\qquad P_W=\sum_i(i+1)D_i^G.$$
- **Adapted orbifold cotangent lattice** ([source](#pm-h-33)): For the rational SNC boundary $B_W=\sum_A b_AA$, its adapted cotangent lattice $\Omega^1(W,B_W)$ has, at a general point of $A=(t=0)$, transverse generator $t^{1-b_A}dt/t$ and ordinary tangential generators.

Write $J=Y_{1,\\eta_{W_1}}$, and suppose that its geometric logarithmic Iitaka dimension is zero. Choose, over $\\mathbb C(W_1)$, $$0\\ne\\omega\\in H^0\\bigl(J,a(K_J+D_J)\\bigr)$$ for some $a>0$. On any further smooth base model $W$, take a smooth resolution $p:Y\\to Y_1$ of the main component, with $x:Y\\to W$, and set $D=p_*^{-1}D_1+\\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\\rm red}$. For a prime $A\\subset W$, put $$\\label{eq:iitaka-minimum} \\begin{aligned} t_A&=\\min_{I\\,\\mapsto\\,A} \\frac{a^{-1}\\operatorname{ord}_I(\\omega)+d_I}{m_I},\\\\ m_I&=\\operatorname{ord}_I(x^*A), \\qquad d_I=\\operatorname{coeff}_I D,\\\\ T_W&=\\sum_A t_AA,\\qquad L_W=K_W+T_W. \\end{aligned}$$ Here $I$ runs through prime divisors dominating $A$, and the order of $\\omega$ is its order as a section of $a(K_Y-x^*K_W)$. The sum defining $T_W$ is finite. For every sufficiently divisible $l>0$, multiplication gives an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:iitaka-exact-sections} \\begin{split} H^0(W,lL_W)&\\xrightarrow{\\ \\simeq\\ } H^0\\bigl(Y_1,l(K_{Y_1}+D_1)\\bigr),\\\\ \\sigma&\\longmapsto \\omega^{l/a}x^*\\sigma. \\end{split}$$ The right side is interpreted on the fixed model $Y_1$, by rational identification. The isomorphisms respect multiplication in a common Veronese subring and preserve ratios.*",

| q-total-kodaira-upper-bound | \kappa(X,L_X) | ≤ | $\kappa(Y,L_Y)+\kappa(F,L_F)$ | The fiber is very general in the original open base. |

| q-zero-base-total-fiber-growth | \kappa(X,L_X) | ≤ | $\kappa(F,L_F)$ | Restriction to the chosen very general fiber in every positive degree. |
| q-original-vector-zero-order | Vanishing order of the original Hodge vector coefficients | ≥ | $e/m$ | Orders use the pulled-back original source frame. |
| q-homogeneous-vector-zero-order | Vanishing order of homogeneous replacement coefficients | ≥ | $ae/m$ | Positive homogeneous degree and original source frame. |
| q-curvature-numerical-dimension | \nu(L) | = | $\max_{z\in Z^\circ}\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}\Theta_z$ | Curvature on the boundary complement; generic rank additionally requires real analyticity. |
| q-horizontal-closure-dimension | \dim R | ≥ | $\dim U+\dim H$ | The irreducible horizontal analytic germ has Zariski-dense projection. |
| q-orbit-filling-dimension | \dim Z-\dim T | = | $\dim(H_{\mathbb C}\cdot a)$ | A local fiber at a Hodge-generic point, at the suitable smooth generic points specified in the lemma. |
| q-marked-rank-splitting | r | = | $d_H+s_Q$ | The rational normal monodromy factor and its complementary period quotient. |
| q-strict-boundary-drop | \nu(\mathcal H\|_{D_i}) | < | $\nu(\mathcal H)$ | Every boundary prime with nonidentity retained Lie monodromy. |
| q-higgs-tail-numerical-invariance | \nu\bigl(P+\pi^*h^*\mathcal H\bigr) | = | $\nu(P)$ | Any dominant connected-generic-fiber pullback with a compatible highest Hodge line. |
| q-relative-iitaka-image-dimension | \dim W-\dim S | = | $k$ | The generic period fiber's logarithmic Iitaka dimension. |
| q-discriminant-coefficient-lower-bound | \operatorname{coeff}_A B_W | ≥ | $1-\frac{1-d_I}{m_I}$ | The same source prime attaining the section-lattice minimum. |
| q-discriminant-coefficient-upper-bound | B_W | ≤ | $1$ | Coefficientwise comparison of the effective SNC discriminant boundary. |
| q-discrepancy-sheaf-rank | Discrepancy-sheaf direct-image rank | = | $1$ | Generic rank, with discrepancy coefficients equal to negative one omitted as stated. |
| q-tail-pseudoeffective-domination | Q\pi^*L_W | ≥ | $P$ | Comparison is pseudoeffective; the positive constant depends only on the fixed maps. |
| q-relative-pseudoeffective-difference | L_W-h^*K_S | ≥ | $0$ | Pseudoeffective comparison after the stated birational preparation. |
| q-positive-period-base-dimension | \kappa(Y,L_Y) | ≥ | $\dim W$ | The marked-period quotient has positive dimension. |
| q-logarithmic-subadditivity-lower-bound | \kappa(Z,K_Z+B_Z) | ≥ | $\kappa(T,K_T+B_T)+\kappa(G,K_G+B_G)$ | The restricted pair on a very general smooth fiber. |

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-log-metric",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a smooth projective variety of dimension $n$, let $\\Delta$ be an SNC divisor, and let $L$ be a rational line bundle. Suppose that on $Z^\\circ=Z\\setminus\\Delta$ it has a smooth Hermitian metric with semipositive curvature form $\\Theta$, normalized to represent $c_1(L)$. Suppose that in regular nonvanishing local frames of a Cartier multiple of $L$ the metric weights satisfy $$\\label{eq:prelim-loglog-bound}\n |\\varphi(z)|\\leq C\\left(1+\n       \\sum_{i=1}^{\\ell}\\log(-\\log|z_i|)\\right)$$ near every boundary chart $\\Delta=\\{z_1\\cdots z_\\ell=0\\}$. Then $L$ is nef. For every Kähler form $\\eta$ on $Z$ and $0\\leq k\\leq n$, $$\\label{eq:prelim-mass-identity}\n \\int_{Z^\\circ}\\Theta^k\\wedge\\eta^{n-k}\n       =c_1(L)^k[\\eta]^{n-k}.$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:prelim-curvature-rank}\n \\nu(L)=\\max_{z\\in Z^\\circ}\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}\\Theta_z.$$ When the curvature is real analytic, as for the induced Hodge and subspace metrics used below, this maximum is its generic rank.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.3"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "q-curvature-numerical-dimension",
      "kind": "bound",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-orbit-rank",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B^\\circ$ be a smooth connected algebraic base carrying the marked data above, with Zariski-dense connected monodromy $G$. The same conclusions hold for their pullback by a dominant algebraic map with connected generic fiber. Consider either the joint flag map $j$, or an algebraic projective line section $\\ell$ of a flat representation of $G$.*\n\n\n**Local orbit filling.* Let $T$ be a local fiber at a Hodge-generic point and let $Z$ be its irreducible Zariski closure. After passing to a finite level on a smooth open of $Z$, its connected monodromy is a rational normal factor $H$ of $G$. Write $G=H\\times Q$. The full $Q$ period is constant on the lifted $Z$. In the flag case the $Q$ compact marks are constant there as well. At suitable smooth generic points of $T$, writing $a$ for $j$ or $\\ell$, one has $$\\label{eq:boundary-orbit-fill}\n \\dim Z-\\dim T=\\dim(H_{\\mathbb C}\\cdot a),\\qquad\n da(T_zZ)=T_{a(z)}(H_{\\mathbb C}\\cdot a(z)).$$ In the flag case write $J=J_H\\times J_Q$ for the products of its $H$ and $Q$ flag factors, and $j=(j_H,j_Q)$ for the corresponding projections in local flat frames. The orbit in <a href=\"#eq:boundary-orbit-fill\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:boundary-orbit-fill\">[eq:boundary-orbit-fill]</a> is then $J_H$.*\n\n\n\n**Flag ranks and lines.* For the flag map on a level of $S$, let $q:B\\to S_Q$ denote a resolved connected-fiber quotient for the full $Q$ period together with its compact marks, and set $s_Q=\\dim S_Q$ and $d_H=\\dim J_H$. If $r=\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}(dj)$, then $$\\label{eq:boundary-rank-split}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}(dj_Q)=s_Q,\\qquad r=d_H+s_Q.$$ On suitable smooth projective models, with $\\rho:B\\to S$ the level map, the nef lines split as $$\\label{eq:boundary-line-split}\n \\rho^*\\mathcal H=\\mathcal H_H+\\mathcal H_Q,\n \\qquad \\nu(\\mathcal H_H)=d_H,\n \\qquad \\mathcal H_Q=q^*\\underline{\\mathcal H}_Q,\n \\qquad \\nu(\\underline{\\mathcal H}_Q)=s_Q.$$ Here all line equalities are rational line-bundle equalities.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.3"
        }
      ],

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-tail-rank",
          "exact_quote": "Let $h:W\\to S$ be any dominant morphism of smooth connected projective varieties with connected generic fiber. On a dense algebraic open of $W$, suppose that $\\ell\\subset h^*E_0$ is an algebraic line subbundle. Require that the pointwise instability-parabolic construction of [lem:marked-flags](#lem:marked-flags), applied to $\\ell$, gives exactly the pulled-back compact marks $h^*j_{\\rm u}$. This compatibility is part of the hypothesis.*\n\n\n*Take a smooth connected unipotent level $\\pi:\\widehat W\\to W$ dominating the level used to define $\\mathcal H$, and resolve its boundary and the generated subspace maps. Over the variation locus, let $G_i$ be the span of all contractions of $\\theta^i(\\ell)$ in $\\pi^*h^*\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}_F^{p_*-i}\\mathbb A$, with $G_0=\\ell$. Use the resulting subbundles of the extended Hodge grades on $\\widehat W$. Put $$\\label{eq:tail-line-definition}\n U_j=\\bigoplus_{i\\geq j}G_i,\n \\qquad\n P=-\\sum_{j\\geq0}\\det U_j\n   =-\\sum_{i\\geq0}(i+1)\\det G_i.$$ Only finitely many summands are nonzero. Then $P$ is nef and $$\\label{eq:tail-rank-equality}\n              \\nu\\bigl(P+\\pi^*h^*\\mathcal H\\bigr)=\\nu(P).$$ The assertion applies to every such $h$ and every such compatible line; $\\ell$ need not be the original vector used to construct $S$.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.3"
        }
      ],

          "exact_quote": "For $q:(Y_0,D_{Y_0})\\to S$ and $u_*$ as above, there are smooth projective varieties $Y,W$, a birational morphism $p:Y\\to Y_0$, and morphisms $x:Y\\to W$ and $h:W\\to S$ with geometrically connected generic fibers, such that $h\\circ x=q\\circ p$. Put $D=p_*^{-1}D_{Y_0}+\\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\\rm red}$ and $k=\\kappa(Y_{0,\\eta_S},K_{Y_{0,\\eta_S}}+D_{Y_0,\\eta_S})$. Then $k\\ge0$, $\\dim W-\\dim S=k$, and the geometric generic fiber of $x$ has logarithmic Iitaka dimension zero. Write $J=Y_{\\eta_W}$ for its generic fiber over $\\mathbb C(W)$.*\n\n\n*The degree $a$ used for $u_*$ itself has a nonzero generator $\\omega\\in H^0(J,a(K_J+D_J))$. There is a rational tensor $u_W\\in aK_W\\otimes h^*E_0$ for which $$\\label{eq:iitaka-vector-factorization}\n u_*=\\omega\\,x^*u_W.$$",

        }

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-rank-one-base",
          "exact_quote": "Take the diagram and degree-$a$ generator $\\omega$ of [lem:relative-iitaka-construction](#lem:relative-iitaka-construction). After further smooth birational preparation, with the reduced source boundary $D=p_*^{-1}D_{Y_0}+\\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\\rm red}$, the divisor $L_W$ defined by <a href=\"#eq:iitaka-minimum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:iitaka-minimum\">[eq:iitaka-minimum]</a> is big over $S$. In every sufficiently divisible degree it satisfies the exact section comparison $$\\label{eq:iitaka-fixed-source}\n H^0(W,lL_W)\\simeq\n H^0\\bigl(Y_0,l(K_{Y_0}+D_{Y_0})\\bigr).$$ Moreover, the models can be chosen so that $$\\label{eq:iitaka-bundle-formula}\n L_W=K_W+T_W=K_W+B_W+M_W,\n \\qquad 0\\le B_W\\le1,\n \\qquad M_W\\ \\text{nef},$$ where $B_W$ has simple-normal-crossing support and the equality uses compatible representatives of the rational divisor classes. If $I$ attains the minimum at a prime $A$, then $$\\label{eq:iitaka-discriminant-bound}\n \\operatorname{coeff}_A B_W\n       \\ge 1-\\frac{1-d_I}{m_I}\\ge0.$$*\n\n*After any further birational preparation of the diagram, the minimum rule can be recomputed with the higher reduced log boundary. The section comparison with the same fixed $(Y_0,D_{Y_0})$ still holds, and the moduli divisor is the trace of the same b-divisor. Once that b-divisor descends to the chosen model, all these higher traces are pullbacks of $M_W$. A further log resolution restores the simple-normal-crossing condition on $B_W$.",

        }
