<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-weight-extension** [claim](#stmt-lem-weight-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weight-extension): Horizontal residues, semistable logarithmic comparison and constant-rank extension of pure Gysin maps. -> Locally free weight subbundles and regular pure weight projection.
- **step-logarithmic-vector** [claim](#stmt-prop-logarithmic-vector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-logarithmic-vector): Cyclic root cover, pure weight projection, coherent connecting maps and transverse semistable coefficient tests. -> Highest Hodge vector with regular coefficients for all iterates and homogeneous replacements.
- **step-original-zero-test** [claim](#stmt-lem-zero-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-zero-test): Pull back the original source frame, divide the ramified root's scalar zero before pure projection, then restore it. -> Positive coefficient vanishing in the original frame, preserved by homogeneous replacement.
- **step-monodromy** [claim](#stmt-lem-marked-monodromy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-monodromy): Connected monodromy normality, fixed tensor invariants, Hodge-circle conjugacy and Higgs integrability. -> Semisimple connected monodromy and compatible Lie variation.
- **step-homogeneous-replacement** [claim](#stmt-lem-marked-replacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-replacement): Irreducible projection, finite-orbit tensoring, Cartan component and tensor power eliminating finite scalar action. -> Minimal homogeneous nonzero replacement factoring through the retained full Lie action.
- **step-compact-flags** [claim](#stmt-lem-marked-flags) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-flags): Minimality excludes nonzero invariant projection on invisible factors; optimal instability parabolics give rational algebraic flag sections. -> Equivariant compact flags and positive invariant Plücker lines.
- **step-generic-stabilizer** [claim](#stmt-lem-generic-lie-stabilizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-generic-lie-stabilizer): A Hodge endomorphism commutes with the generic Mumford–Tate group and inner monodromy; centerlessness gives identity. -> Trivial rational filtration-preserving Lie stabilizer.
- **step-period-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-period-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-period-descent): Integral period-image algebraicity, relative normalization, vertical monodromy elimination and compact-flag image construction. -> Connected algebraic quotient with actual descended variations and immersive joint differential.
- **step-metric-mass** [claim](#stmt-lem-log-metric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-log-metric): Extend plurisubharmonic weights, prove zero Lelong numbers, compare with full-mass barriers and identify mixed polynomial coefficients. -> Nefness, full mixed intersection mass and curvature-rank numerical dimension.
- **step-initial-adjoint** [claim](#stmt-lem-initial-adjoint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-initial-adjoint): Curvature of the joint differential image, compact-flag compensation, logarithmic tangent determinant comparison and finite norm descent. -> Initial logarithmic adjoint bigness.
- **step-horizontal-dimension** [claim](#stmt-lem-connection-dimension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-connection-dimension): Constancy of the connection-image Grassmann map along a dense horizontal germ and exclusion of a proper algebraic monodromy reduction. -> Lower bound for the dimension of the germ's algebraic closure.
- **step-orbit-ranks** [claim](#stmt-lem-orbit-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-orbit-rank): Normal-factor monodromy of fiber closures, algebraic stabilizer incidence and horizontal dimension comparison. -> Orbit filling, complementary-period constancy and nef-line rank splitting.
- **step-boundary-drop** [claim](#stmt-prop-boundary-drop) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-boundary-drop): Split normal-factor contributions; use primitive limiting weight lines for infinite monodromy and fixed-flag obstruction for finite monodromy. -> Strict numerical-dimension loss on each marked boundary component.
- **step-boundary-section-estimate** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-remove) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-remove): Successive restriction sequences and Hilbert polynomial comparison in the nef parameter. -> Bigness after sufficiently large divisible nef twist and fixed boundary subtraction.
- **step-adjoint-bigness** [claim](#stmt-prop-adjoint-bigness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-adjoint-bigness): Subtract the marked boundary from an ample piece of the initial big adjoint, then add the remaining effective divisor. -> Boundary-free adjoint bigness for sufficiently positive marked twist.
- **step-marked-construction** [claim](#stmt-prop-marked-period) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-period-descent): Assemble homogeneous replacement, compact flags, actual variation descent, nef metric lines and both adjoint positivity comparisons. -> The marked-period quotient and all its asserted descent and positivity properties.
- **step-degree-one-roots** [claim](#stmt-lem-degree-one-root) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-degree-one-root-2): Degree-one root blocks, projective incidence, a Borel fixed pair and highest-weight root-string annihilation. -> Adjoint lowering annihilates the entire highest graded piece.
- **step-higgs-tail-rank** [claim](#stmt-prop-tail-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-degree-one-root): Higgs-invariant tail curvature, projective-line orbit filling and degree-one root annihilation yield curvature-kernel inclusion. -> Nef weighted tail line and unchanged numerical dimension after adding the marked line.
- **step-relative-iitaka** [claim](#stmt-lem-relative-iitaka-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-relative-iitaka-construction): Nonzero generic scalar coordinate, complete relative section system, Stein factorization and section-ratio maximality. -> Relative Iitaka diagram and factorization by the generator in the original degree.
- **step-source-valuations** [claim](#stmt-lem-valuation-preparation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-valuation-preparation): Extract restricted valuations of contracted vertical source primes, then impose the minimum order test on each fixed-source prime. -> Exact multiplicative divisible section-lattice comparison preserving ratios.
- **step-rank-one-base** [claim](#stmt-prop-rank-one-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rank-one-base): Exact valuations, auxiliary subpair, explicit discrepancy-sheaf rank verification, discriminant thresholds and moduli b-nefness. -> Relatively big intermediate divisor, original-source section equality, effective SNC discriminant and nef moduli part.
- **step-nef-subtraction** [claim](#stmt-lem-nef-morse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nef-morse): Mixed nef-intersection vanishing and a strict Morse section estimate from successive ample restrictions. -> Bigness after fixed nef subtraction and sufficiently large nef scaling.
- **step-twist-removal** [claim](#stmt-prop-twist-removal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nef-morse-2): Make the twisted divisor big using relative bigness, then apply fixed nef domination and nef subtraction on a generically finite cover. -> Bigness of the original divisor without the period twist.
- **step-orbifold-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-orbifold-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-orbifold-transfer): Galois-invariant determinant descent, negativity lemma and coefficient order comparison using the same minimizing source prime. -> Pseudoeffective downstairs tail divisor and regular determinant maps into adapted cotangent tensors.
- **step-uniform-threshold** [claim](#stmt-lem-pseff-threshold) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pseff-threshold): Ample moduli perturbations, orbifold cotangent quotient positivity, movable-cone duality and closed-cone threshold contradiction. -> Pseudoeffectivity of the intermediate divisor and fixed positive domination of the nef tail.
- **step-relative-pseudoeffectivity** [claim](#stmt-lem-nonvanishing-relative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nonvanishing-relative): Prepare a good fibration model, apply relative orbifold positivity to perturbed full boundaries, push forward and remove the perturbation. -> Relative pseudoeffectivity on the fixed intermediate base.
- **step-positive-period** [claim](#stmt-prop-positive-period) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-positive-period): Apply period-twist removal and return the complete divisible section systems through the exact lattice. -> Intermediate bigness and positive original-base logarithmic Kodaira dimension.
- **step-base-nonvanishing** [claim](#stmt-prop-nonvanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-positive-period-2): For a point quotient use nonzero constant-frame coordinates and the original zero test; for positive-dimensional quotient use intermediate bigness. -> Base nonvanishing, prescribed original-point zeros in the point case, and positive dimension in the other case.
- **step-section-construction** [claim](#stmt-prop-section-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-positive-period-3): Base nonvanishing excludes the negative-base case; zero base Kodaira dimension excludes the positive-period alternative. -> The required nonzero base pluriform, with the prescribed zero when the base dimension is zero.
- **step-reverse-inequality** [claim](#stmt-thm-upper-bound) / [proof](#sec:completion): Treat both negative-infinity cases, prove degreewise restriction injectivity for zero base dimension, and reduce positive base dimension through its logarithmic Iitaka fibration. -> The reverse logarithmic Kodaira inequality in every sign case.
- **step-companion-lower-bound** [claim](#stmt-thm-log-iitaka) / [proof](#sec:matched-equality): Invoke the recorded companion logarithmic subadditivity theorem with the same reduced pairs and support inclusion. -> The matching lower logarithmic Kodaira inequality.
- **step-additivity** [claim](#stmt-cor-additivity) / [proof](#sec:matched-equality): Match the base, total space, very general fiber and infinity conventions in the two inequalities. -> Logarithmic Kodaira additivity under stratum smoothness.

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-log-metric",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-log-metric",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a smooth projective variety of dimension $n$, let $\\Delta$ be an SNC divisor, and let $L$ be a rational line bundle. Suppose that on $Z^\\circ=Z\\setminus\\Delta$ it has a smooth Hermitian metric with semipositive curvature form $\\Theta$, normalized to represent $c_1(L)$. Suppose that in regular nonvanishing local frames of a Cartier multiple of $L$ the metric weights satisfy $$\\label{eq:prelim-loglog-bound}\n |\\varphi(z)|\\leq C\\left(1+\n       \\sum_{i=1}^{\\ell}\\log(-\\log|z_i|)\\right)$$ near every boundary chart $\\Delta=\\{z_1\\cdots z_\\ell=0\\}$. Then $L$ is nef. For every Kähler form $\\eta$ on $Z$ and $0\\leq k\\leq n$, $$\\label{eq:prelim-mass-identity}\n \\int_{Z^\\circ}\\Theta^k\\wedge\\eta^{n-k}\n       =c_1(L)^k[\\eta]^{n-k}.$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:prelim-curvature-rank}\n \\nu(L)=\\max_{z\\in Z^\\circ}\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}\\Theta_z.$$ When the curvature is real analytic, as for the induced Hodge and subspace metrics used below, this maximum is its generic rank.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-metric-mass",
      "inputs": [
        "Semipositive smooth curvature on the open set",
        "Two-sided logarithmic-logarithmic weight bound"
      ],
      "method": "Extend plurisubharmonic weights, prove zero Lelong numbers, compare with full-mass barriers and identify mixed polynomial coefficients.",
      "output": "Nefness, full mixed intersection mass and curvature-rank numerical dimension.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-log-metric"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-connection-dimension",

Suppose $\\mathcal P$ carries an algebraic flat principal connection whose monodromy is Zariski dense in $H$. Let $U$ be an irreducible analytic germ contained in a horizontal leaf, and suppose $p(U)$ is Zariski dense in $Z$. If $R$ is the Zariski closure of $U$ in $\\mathcal P$, then $$\\label{eq:prelim-horizontal-dimension}\n \\dim R\\geq\\dim U+\\dim H.$$",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-orbit-rank",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B^\\circ$ be a smooth connected algebraic base carrying the marked data above, with Zariski-dense connected monodromy $G$. The same conclusions hold for their pullback by a dominant algebraic map with connected generic fiber. Consider either the joint flag map $j$, or an algebraic projective line section $\\ell$ of a flat representation of $G$.*\n\n\n**Local orbit filling.* Let $T$ be a local fiber at a Hodge-generic point and let $Z$ be its irreducible Zariski closure. After passing to a finite level on a smooth open of $Z$, its connected monodromy is a rational normal factor $H$ of $G$. Write $G=H\\times Q$. The full $Q$ period is constant on the lifted $Z$. In the flag case the $Q$ compact marks are constant there as well. At suitable smooth generic points of $T$, writing $a$ for $j$ or $\\ell$, one has $$\\label{eq:boundary-orbit-fill}\n \\dim Z-\\dim T=\\dim(H_{\\mathbb C}\\cdot a),\\qquad\n da(T_zZ)=T_{a(z)}(H_{\\mathbb C}\\cdot a(z)).$$ In the flag case write $J=J_H\\times J_Q$ for the products of its $H$ and $Q$ flag factors, and $j=(j_H,j_Q)$ for the corresponding projections in local flat frames. The orbit in <a href=\"#eq:boundary-orbit-fill\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:boundary-orbit-fill\">[eq:boundary-orbit-fill]</a> is then $J_H$.*\n\n\n\n**Flag ranks and lines.* For the flag map on a level of $S$, let $q:B\\to S_Q$ denote a resolved connected-fiber quotient for the full $Q$ period together with its compact marks, and set $s_Q=\\dim S_Q$ and $d_H=\\dim J_H$. If $r=\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}(dj)$, then $$\\label{eq:boundary-rank-split}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}(dj_Q)=s_Q,\\qquad r=d_H+s_Q.$$ On suitable smooth projective models, with $\\rho:B\\to S$ the level map, the nef lines split as $$\\label{eq:boundary-line-split}\n \\rho^*\\mathcal H=\\mathcal H_H+\\mathcal H_Q,\n \\qquad \\nu(\\mathcal H_H)=d_H,\n \\qquad \\mathcal H_Q=q^*\\underline{\\mathcal H}_Q,\n \\qquad \\nu(\\underline{\\mathcal H}_Q)=s_Q.$$ Here all line equalities are rational line-bundle equalities.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.3"
        }
      ],

      "output": "Orbit filling, complementary-period constancy and nef-line rank splitting.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-orbit-rank"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-marked-period",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-homogeneous-replacement",
        "step-compact-flags",
        "step-period-descent",
        "step-metric-mass",

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-marked-period",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B^\\circ$ be a smooth irreducible complex quasi-projective variety, and let $\\mathbb V$ be a polarizable rational pure variation with an invariant integral lattice. Suppose that a line bundle $\\mathcal L$ has a generically nonzero algebraic morphism $$u:\\mathcal L\\longrightarrow F^p\\mathbb V_{\\mathbb C},\n \\qquad F^{p+1}\\mathbb V_{\\mathbb C}=0.$$ There are a positive integer $a$, a homogeneous tensor construction followed by flat Hodge-compatible projections, and a generically nonzero morphism $$\\label{eq:marked-vector}\n u_*:\\mathcal L^{\\otimes a}\\longrightarrow E_0,\n \\qquad E_0=F^{p_*}\\mathbb A,\n \\qquad F^{p_*+1}\\mathbb A=0,$$ where $\\mathbb A$ is a polarized complex pure variation. The operations may first be performed equivariantly at finite monodromy level. They descend to $B^\\circ$ after shrinking it. On smooth projective birational models there is a dominant morphism $b:B\\to S$ with connected fibers and the following properties.*\n\n1.  **Descent.* On dense smooth opens, $\\mathbb A$ is the pullback of an actual variation on $S^\\circ$. Its connected representation is irreducible and factors through a rational semisimple adjoint group $G$. The full monodromy acts through its projected action on $\\mathfrak g=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}G$. In particular, finite scalar monodromy in the kernel of that action has been eliminated.*\n\n2.  **The marks.* There is an algebraic compact-flag section $j_{\\mathrm u}$, possibly empty, determined pointwise and equivariantly by $[u_*]$. In flat coordinates the joint marks $$j=(E_0,j_{\\mathrm u})$$ take values in a closed product flag orbit $J$ of $G_{\\mathbb C}$. The two kinds of marks use disjoint complex simple factors. Every rational simple factor of $G$ has a complex factor acting nontrivially on $J$. The full Lie-period map together with $j_{\\mathrm u}$ is generically immersive on $S^\\circ$.*\n\n\n\n3.  **The nef lines and adjoint positivity.* If $S$ is a point, $\\mathbb A$ is constant and we set $\\mathcal H=\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}=0$. Otherwise $S$ has nef rational line bundles $\\mathcal H$ and $\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}$, with $\\mathcal H-\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}$ nef. On a smooth unipotent connected-monodromy cover their pullbacks are respectively $$\\det E_0+\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}\n     \\quad\\hbox{and the positive compact-flag line.}$$ The generic curvature rank of $\\mathcal H$ is the generic rank of $dj$, and equals $\\nu(\\mathcal H)$. If $D_0$ denotes the reduced union of boundary components with nonidentity local monodromy on $\\mathfrak g$, then $$\\label{eq:marked-initial-big}\n     K_S+D_0+c\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm u}\\quad\\text{is big for }c\\gg0.$$ Furthermore, $$\\label{eq:marked-final-big}\n     K_S+b_0\\mathcal H\\quad\\text{is big for }b_0\\gg0.$$*\n\n*All assertions about extensions are compatible with further smooth projective modifications and pullback at unipotent level. In particular, the iterated Higgs fields of $u_*$ are polynomial tensor expressions in those of $u$; the construction introduces no division by a local function.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-marked-construction",
      "inputs": [
        "Highest Hodge vector and source line",
        "Integral polarized coefficient variation"
      ],
      "method": "Assemble homogeneous replacement, compact flags, actual variation descent, nef metric lines and both adjoint positivity comparisons.",
      "output": "The marked-period quotient and all its asserted descent and positivity properties.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-period-descent"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-degree-one-root",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathfrak g=\\bigoplus_k\\mathfrak g_k$ be a complex semisimple Lie algebra with an integral grading and an adjoint operation $*$ carrying $\\mathfrak g_k$ to $\\mathfrak g_{-k}$, arising from a compatible compact form. Let $\\mathfrak h$ be a graded ideal stable under $*$, and let $V$ be an irreducible finite-dimensional representation with compatible grading and highest graded piece $E$. The metric on $V$ is chosen so that representation adjoints are the actions of Lie adjoints. For $0\\ne s\\in E$ and $x\\in\\mathfrak h_1$, suppose $$\\label{eq:root-annihilation-hypothesis}\n                         [x,\\mathfrak h_{-1}]s=0.$$ Then $x^*E=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.3"
        }

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-tail-rank",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-orbit-ranks",
        "step-degree-one-roots",
        "step-metric-mass"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-tail-rank",
          "exact_quote": "Let $h:W\\to S$ be any dominant morphism of smooth connected projective varieties with connected generic fiber. On a dense algebraic open of $W$, suppose that $\\ell\\subset h^*E_0$ is an algebraic line subbundle. Require that the pointwise instability-parabolic construction of [lem:marked-flags](#lem:marked-flags), applied to $\\ell$, gives exactly the pulled-back compact marks $h^*j_{\\rm u}$. This compatibility is part of the hypothesis.*\n\n\n*Take a smooth connected unipotent level $\\pi:\\widehat W\\to W$ dominating the level used to define $\\mathcal H$, and resolve its boundary and the generated subspace maps. Over the variation locus, let $G_i$ be the span of all contractions of $\\theta^i(\\ell)$ in $\\pi^*h^*\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}_F^{p_*-i}\\mathbb A$, with $G_0=\\ell$. Use the resulting subbundles of the extended Hodge grades on $\\widehat W$. Put $$\\label{eq:tail-line-definition}\n U_j=\\bigoplus_{i\\geq j}G_i,\n \\qquad\n P=-\\sum_{j\\geq0}\\det U_j\n   =-\\sum_{i\\geq0}(i+1)\\det G_i.$$ Only finitely many summands are nonzero. Then $P$ is nef and $$\\label{eq:tail-rank-equality}\n              \\nu\\bigl(P+\\pi^*h^*\\mathcal H\\bigr)=\\nu(P).$$ The assertion applies to every such $h$ and every such compatible line; $\\ell$ need not be the original vector used to construct $S$.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.3"
        }
      ],

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-degree-one-root"

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-relative-iitaka-construction",
          "exact_quote": "For $q:(Y_0,D_{Y_0})\\to S$ and $u_*$ as above, there are smooth projective varieties $Y,W$, a birational morphism $p:Y\\to Y_0$, and morphisms $x:Y\\to W$ and $h:W\\to S$ with geometrically connected generic fibers, such that $h\\circ x=q\\circ p$. Put $D=p_*^{-1}D_{Y_0}+\\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\\rm red}$ and $k=\\kappa(Y_{0,\\eta_S},K_{Y_{0,\\eta_S}}+D_{Y_0,\\eta_S})$. Then $k\\ge0$, $\\dim W-\\dim S=k$, and the geometric generic fiber of $x$ has logarithmic Iitaka dimension zero. Write $J=Y_{\\eta_W}$ for its generic fiber over $\\mathbb C(W)$.*\n\n\n*The degree $a$ used for $u_*$ itself has a nonzero generator $\\omega\\in H^0(J,a(K_J+D_J))$. There is a rational tensor $u_W\\in aK_W\\otimes h^*E_0$ for which $$\\label{eq:iitaka-vector-factorization}\n u_*=\\omega\\,x^*u_W.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-033.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-relative-iitaka",
      "inputs": [
        "Regular logarithmic homogeneous vector",
        "Descended constant coefficient system on the generic period fiber"
      ],
      "method": "Nonzero generic scalar coordinate, complete relative section system, Stein factorization and section-ratio maximality.",
      "output": "Relative Iitaka diagram and factorization by the generator in the original degree.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-relative-iitaka-construction"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-valuation-preparation",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-valuation-preparation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $x_1:Y_1\\to W_1$ be a dominant projective morphism of smooth projective varieties with geometrically connected generic fiber, and fix a reduced simple-normal-crossing divisor $D_1$ on $Y_1$. Write $J=Y_{1,\\eta_{W_1}}$, and suppose that its geometric logarithmic Iitaka dimension is zero. Choose, over $\\mathbb C(W_1)$, $$0\\ne\\omega\\in H^0\\bigl(J,a(K_J+D_J)\\bigr)$$ for some $a>0$. Regard $\\omega$ as a rational section of $aK_{Y_1/W_1}$. There is a smooth projective modification of $W_1$ with the following property. On any further smooth base model $W$, take a smooth resolution $p:Y\\to Y_1$ of the main component, with $x:Y\\to W$, and set $D=p_*^{-1}D_1+\\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\\rm red}$. For a prime $A\\subset W$, put $$\\label{eq:iitaka-minimum}\n \\begin{aligned}\n t_A&=\\min_{I\\,\\mapsto\\,A}\n       \\frac{a^{-1}\\operatorname{ord}_I(\\omega)+d_I}{m_I},\\\\\n m_I&=\\operatorname{ord}_I(x^*A),\n       \\qquad d_I=\\operatorname{coeff}_I D,\\\\\n T_W&=\\sum_A t_AA,\\qquad L_W=K_W+T_W.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Here $I$ runs through prime divisors dominating $A$, and the order of $\\omega$ is its order as a section of $a(K_Y-x^*K_W)$. The sum defining $T_W$ is finite. For every sufficiently divisible $l>0$, multiplication gives an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:iitaka-exact-sections}\n \\begin{split}\n H^0(W,lL_W)&\\xrightarrow{\\ \\simeq\\ }\n       H^0\\bigl(Y_1,l(K_{Y_1}+D_1)\\bigr),\\\\\n \\sigma&\\longmapsto \\omega^{l/a}x^*\\sigma.\n \\end{split}$$ The right side is interpreted on the fixed model $Y_1$, by rational identification. The isomorphisms respect multiplication in a common Veronese subring and preserve ratios.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-source-valuations",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-rank-one-base",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-rank-one-base",
          "exact_quote": "Take the diagram and degree-$a$ generator $\\omega$ of [lem:relative-iitaka-construction](#lem:relative-iitaka-construction). After further smooth birational preparation, with the reduced source boundary $D=p_*^{-1}D_{Y_0}+\\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\\rm red}$, the divisor $L_W$ defined by <a href=\"#eq:iitaka-minimum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:iitaka-minimum\">[eq:iitaka-minimum]</a> is big over $S$. In every sufficiently divisible degree it satisfies the exact section comparison $$\\label{eq:iitaka-fixed-source}\n H^0(W,lL_W)\\simeq\n H^0\\bigl(Y_0,l(K_{Y_0}+D_{Y_0})\\bigr).$$ Moreover, the models can be chosen so that $$\\label{eq:iitaka-bundle-formula}\n L_W=K_W+T_W=K_W+B_W+M_W,\n \\qquad 0\\le B_W\\le1,\n \\qquad M_W\\ \\text{nef},$$ where $B_W$ has simple-normal-crossing support and the equality uses compatible representatives of the rational divisor classes. If $I$ attains the minimum at a prime $A$, then $$\\label{eq:iitaka-discriminant-bound}\n \\operatorname{coeff}_A B_W\n       \\ge 1-\\frac{1-d_I}{m_I}\\ge0.$$*\n\n*After any further birational preparation of the diagram, the minimum rule can be recomputed with the higher reduced log boundary. The section comparison with the same fixed $(Y_0,D_{Y_0})$ still holds, and the moduli divisor is the trace of the same b-divisor. Once that b-divisor descends to the chosen model, all these higher traces are pullbacks of $M_W$. A further log resolution restores the simple-normal-crossing condition on $B_W$.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.3"
        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-nef-morse",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be smooth projective of dimension $n>0$, let $A$ be an ample rational divisor, and let $P,H$ be nef rational divisors satisfying $\\nu(P+H)=\\nu(P)$. For every fixed rational $b\\ge0$, the divisor $A+tP-bH$ is big for all sufficiently large rational $t$.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-twist-removal",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-nef-subtraction"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-twist-removal",
          "exact_quote": "Let $h:W\\to S$ be a surjective morphism of smooth projective varieties, with $\\dim S>0$. Let $L$ be a rational divisor on $W$ and $H$ a nef rational divisor on $S$. <a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-reverse-logarithmic-inequality**: The total logarithmic Kodaira dimension is bounded above by the sum of the base and very general fiber logarithmic Kodaira dimensions. Negative infinity in either summand forces vanishing of every positive-degree total-space logarithmic pluriform space.
- **invariant-original-point-zero**: A nonzero total-space logarithmic pluriform forces base nonvanishing. When the base logarithmic Kodaira dimension is zero, vanishing on a fiber over the original open base produces a nonzero base pluriform vanishing at that same point.
- **invariant-original-coefficient-lattice**: The highest Hodge vector and its Higgs iterates have regular coefficients in the prescribed logarithmic differential lattice on every stated higher model. Homogeneous tensor operations and flat Hodge-compatible projections preserve this regularity.
- **invariant-compatible-higgs-tail-rank**: The weighted determinant line formed from the generated Higgs tails is nef. Adding the pulled-back marked nef line preserves its numerical dimension when the chosen line induces exactly the prescribed compact marks.
- **invariant-nef-subtraction**: An ample divisor plus a sufficiently large multiple of the first nef divisor remains big after subtracting a fixed nonnegative rational multiple of the second, provided their sum has the same numerical dimension as the first.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-unverified-geometric-hypotheses**: Apply the reverse inequality to arbitrary complex fibrations or to boundaries whose strata become singular over the open base. -> The morphism or its boundary strata may fail the hypotheses needed for the logarithmic coefficient construction.
- **anti-use-arbitrary-special-fiber**: Replace the very general fiber invariant in the reverse inequality by the invariant of a selected special fiber. -> The selected fiber need not have the very general section dimensions used in the restriction and growth argument.
- **anti-use-zero-construction-without-zero-base**: Use the section-construction proposition to obtain a prescribed vanishing base section for every nonzero total-space section regardless of the base logarithmic Kodaira dimension. -> The nonvanishing conclusion alone does not supply the proposition's prescribed-zero conclusion in the unrestricted base regime.
- **anti-use-testing-curve-cotangent-substitution**: Replace the external base cotangent tensors by cotangent tensors on a transverse testing curve. -> Tangential tensor factors can disappear, and transverse differential substitutions can introduce divisions that do not belong to the coefficient test.
- **anti-use-unmatched-compact-marks**: Apply the Higgs-tail numerical-dimension equality to an arbitrary highest Hodge line. -> The chosen line can induce different compact marks, invalidating the comparison with the fixed marked nef line.
- **anti-use-identifying-distinct-period-ranks**: Identify the full marked-period quotient dimension with the curvature rank of the marked nef line without proving the required comparison. -> The full Lie-period differential can detect directions missed by the highest-space and compact-flag marks.
- **anti-use-effective-threshold-from-existence**: Treat the nef subtraction statement as an executable rule returning an explicit sufficient scaling parameter. -> The statement guarantees eventual bigness but supplies no numerical threshold or finite algorithm for evaluating the required geometric intersection data.
- **anti-use-full-theorem-from-finite-tests**: Certify the logarithmic inequality or universal section vanishing by testing finitely many varieties, fibers, or degrees. -> Finite agreement does not decide very general fiber behavior or the universal positive-degree vanishing conclusion.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The source proves geometric existence and universal pluriform statements, rather than a finite constructive computation. Its restriction argument requires: "Otherwise choose $y\in V$ very general so that all fiber pluriform spaces have their very general dimensions and $y$ lies outside the zero divisor of every nonzero base pluriform." The Hodge and relative Iitaka constructions provide no finite input encoding or effective procedure deciding the asserted Kodaira dimensions. Finite enumeration cannot certify the very general point choice or vanishing in every positive degree.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The vanishing conclusion can support a conditional mathematical inference, but the paper does not supply an executable procedure for checking the geometric hypotheses or deciding the required Kodaira dimensions. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The setting consists of complex projective varieties and logarithmic boundaries; it supplies no evolution rule, numerical simulation scheme, or game mechanic. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The compact-flag construction produces a generically algebraic section after restriction to a suitable open set. It does not specify a computational interface for constructing that section from finite input. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The nef-subtraction result establishes eventual bigness for sufficiently large rational parameters without furnishing a computable threshold or a terminating algorithm. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The weight filtration consists of geometric subbundles and pure variation quotients. The paper does not define a finite representation with storage, update, or query operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The homogeneous tensor operations preserve divisorial coefficient regularity. Their role is geometric, and no hardware execution model or acceleration construction is provided. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The generic Lie-stabilizer lemma concerns rational automorphisms preserving a Hodge filtration. It establishes no cryptographic hardness assumption, security reduction, or authentication mechanism. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The curvature-rank identity measures positivity of a rational line bundle under boundary-growth assumptions. It gives no learning model, training objective, or statistical generalization contract. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The boundary-removal argument compares asymptotic section growth and concludes bigness. It does not construct a solver for an optimisation problem with finite feasible solutions. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Descent through a connected-fiber algebraic quotient concerns variations and period data. It specifies no communication protocol, replicated state, or distributed consistency property. |
| other (algebraic and birational geometry) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The relative logarithmic Iitaka construction produces an intermediate base and factors the chosen Hodge vector through a generic-fiber generator. Valuation preparation then identifies complete divisible section spaces with those on the fixed source. This is a mathematical construction suitable for adaptation into a symbolic or formal development; the paper supplies no finite executable construction. |

          "exact_quote": "For $q:(Y_0,D_{Y_0})\\to S$ and $u_*$ as above, there are smooth projective varieties $Y,W$, a birational morphism $p:Y\\to Y_0$, and morphisms $x:Y\\to W$ and $h:W\\to S$ with geometrically connected generic fibers, such that $h\\circ x=q\\circ p$. Then $k\\ge0$, $\\dim W-\\dim S=k$, and the geometric generic fiber of $x$ has logarithmic Iitaka dimension zero. Write $J=Y_{\\eta_W}$ for its generic fiber over $\\mathbb C(W)$.*\n\n\n*The degree $a$ used for $u_*$ itself has a nonzero generator $\\omega\\in H^0(J,a(K_J+D_J))$. There is a rational tensor $u_W\\in aK_W\\otimes h^*E_0$ for which $$\\label{eq:iitaka-vector-factorization}\n u_*=\\omega\\,x^*u_W.$$",

Regard $\\omega$ as a rational section of $aK_{Y_1/W_1}$. There is a smooth projective modification of $W_1$ with the following property. On any further smooth base model $W$, take a smooth resolution $p:Y\\to Y_1$ of the main component, with $x:Y\\to W$, and set $D=p_*^{-1}D_1+\\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\\rm red}$. For a prime $A\\subset W$, put $$\\label{eq:iitaka-minimum}\n \\begin{aligned}\n t_A&=\\min_{I\\,\\mapsto\\,A}\n       \\frac{a^{-1}\\operatorname{ord}_I(\\omega)+d_I}{m_I},\\\\\n m_I&=\\operatorname{ord}_I(x^*A),\n       \\qquad d_I=\\operatorname{coeff}_I D,\\\\\n T_W&=\\sum_A t_AA,\\qquad L_W=K_W+T_W.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Here $I$ runs through prime divisors dominating $A$, and the order of $\\omega$ is its order as a section of $a(K_Y-x^*K_W)$. The sum defining $T_W$ is finite. For every sufficiently divisible $l>0$, multiplication gives an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:iitaka-exact-sections}\n \\begin{split}\n H^0(W,lL_W)&\\xrightarrow{\\ \\simeq\\ }\n       H^0\\bigl(Y_1,l(K_{Y_1}+D_1)\\bigr),\\\\\n \\sigma&\\longmapsto \\omega^{l/a}x^*\\sigma.\n \\end{split}$$ The right side is interpreted on the fixed model $Y_1$, by rational identification. In every sufficiently divisible degree it satisfies the exact section comparison $$\\label{eq:iitaka-fixed-source}\n H^0(W,lL_W)\\simeq\n H^0\\bigl(Y_0,l(K_{Y_0}+D_{Y_0})\\bigr).$$ Moreover, the models can be chosen so that $$\\label{eq:iitaka-bundle-formula}\n L_W=K_W+T_W=K_W+B_W+M_W,\n \\qquad 0\\le B_W\\le1,\n \\qquad M_W\\ \\text{nef},$$ where $B_W$ has simple-normal-crossing support and the equality uses compatible representatives of the rational divisor classes. If $I$ attains the minimum at a prime $A$, then $$\\label{eq:iitaka-discriminant-bound}\n \\operatorname{coeff}_A B_W\n       \\ge 1-\\frac{1-d_I}{m_I}\\ge0.$$*\n\n*After any further birational preparation of the diagram, the minimum rule can be recomputed with the higher reduced log boundary. The section comparison with the same fixed $(Y_0,D_{Y_0})$ still holds, and the moduli divisor is the trace of the same b-divisor. Once that b-divisor descends to the chosen model, all these higher traces are pullbacks of $M_W$. A further log resolution restores the simple-normal-crossing condition on $B_W$.",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-complex-projective-connected-setting** (hypothesis): The reverse inequality concerns smooth connected projective complex varieties and a surjective morphism with connected fibers. Blocked use: ['Apply the reverse inequality to positive-characteristic varieties, nonprojective complex manifolds, or morphisms with disconnected fibers.']
- **limit-reduced-snc-support-and-strata** (hypothesis): The boundaries must be reduced SNC, satisfy the support inclusion, and have all the specified source strata smooth over the original open base. The support condition does not discard pullback multiplicities. Blocked use: ['Apply the upper inequality to nonreduced boundaries or boundary strata singular over the open base.', 'Replace the support inclusion by an equality of divisors or ignore pullback multiplicities.']
- **limit-very-general-fiber** (formal_scope): The fiber invariant in the upper inequality belongs to the very general fiber pair. The prescribed-point zero assertion has a different scope and does not replace this fiber regime. Blocked use: ['Substitute the logarithmic Kodaira dimension of an arbitrarily selected special fiber into the main inequality.']
- **limit-prescribed-zero-conditional** (hypothesis): The section-construction proposition requires a nonzero total pluriform. Its prescribed-zero conclusion additionally requires zero base logarithmic Kodaira dimension and fiberwise vanishing at a point of the original open base. Blocked use: ['Infer a prescribed vanishing base section from base nonvanishing alone.', 'Apply the prescribed-zero clause at a point of the base boundary.']
- **limit-root-cover-original-lattice** (formal_scope): The auxiliary root cover can have additional discriminant inside the original open base. Coefficient regularity still uses the specified pulled-back base tensor frames; the testing curve does not replace the external cotangent bundle. No effective search bound for these choices is supplied. Blocked use: ['Claim a terminating general procedure for finding the tensor replacement or an Iitaka system from finite equation data.']
- **limit-metric-growth-and-semipositivity** (hypothesis): The metric comparison needs semipositive curvature and the stated two-sided boundary weight control. - **limit-boundary-drop-nonidentity-monodromy** (formal_scope): Strict boundary drop concerns the marked boundary with nonidentity retained Lie monodromy, including finite nonidentity monodromy. ### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 033 (`lean/docs/033.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The reverse logarithmic Kodaira inequality and additivity](../../preprints/The-reverse-logarithmic-Kodaira-inequality-and-additivity-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The paper studies logarithmic Kodaira additivity for connected-fiber morphisms of smooth projective reduced simple-normal-crossing pairs that are smooth on all boundary strata away from the base boundary. The linked formalization proves the negative-fiber branch: for a very general base point, if the logarithmic Kodaira dimension of the fiber is $-\infty$, then the total logarithmic Kodaira dimension equals the sum of the base and fiber dimensions and is $-\infty$. Every positive-degree logarithmic pluriform section on the total space then vanishes.
>
> The finite-dimension and negative-base branches of the paper's additivity theorem are outside this selected statement.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

 

noncomputable section

open CategoryTheory AlgebraicGeometry
open scoped TensorProduct

namespace ReverseLogKodaira

attribute [local instance] MvPolynomial.gradedAlgebra

abbrev complexBase : Scheme := Spec (CommRingCat.of ℂ)

abbrev projectiveGrading (N : ℕ) := MvPolynomial.homogeneousSubmodule (Fin (N + 1)) ℂ

abbrev complexProjectiveSpace (N : ℕ) : Scheme := Proj (projectiveGrading N)

def projectiveConstants (N : ℕ) : ℂ →+* projectiveGrading N 0 where
  toFun r := ⟨MvPolynomial.C r, MvPolynomial.isHomogeneous_C (Fin (N + 1)) r⟩
  map_zero' := Subtype.ext (map_zero MvPolynomial.C)
  map_one' := Subtype.ext (map_one MvPolynomial.C)
  map_add' x y := Subtype.ext (map_add MvPolynomial.C x y)
  map_mul' x y := Subtype.ext (map_mul MvPolynomial.C x y)

def complexProjectiveStructure (N : ℕ) : complexProjectiveSpace N ⟶ complexBase :=
  Proj.toSpecZero (projectiveGrading N) ≫ Spec.map (CommRingCat.ofHom (projectiveConstants N))

 
structure SmoothProjectiveVariety where
  scheme : Scheme
  structural : scheme ⟶ complexBase
  dimension : ℕ
  integral : IsIntegral scheme
  smooth : SmoothOfRelativeDimension dimension structural
  projective : ∃ (N : ℕ) (i : scheme ⟶ complexProjectiveSpace N),
    IsClosedImmersion i ∧ i ≫ complexProjectiveStructure N = structural

attribute [instance] SmoothProjectiveVariety.integral SmoothProjectiveVariety.smooth

namespace SmoothProjectiveVariety

instance (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) : Smooth X.structural :=
  SmoothOfRelativeDimension.smooth X.dimension X.structural

 
def globalConstants (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) : ℂ →+* Γ(X.scheme, ⊤) :=
  X.structural.appTop.hom.comp (Scheme.ΓSpecIso (CommRingCat.of ℂ)).inv.hom

 
def constants (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) (U : X.scheme.Opens) : ℂ →+* Γ(X.scheme, U) :=
  (X.scheme.presheaf.map (homOfLE (le_top (a := U))).op).hom.comp X.globalConstants

instance (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) (U : X.scheme.Opens) : Algebra ℂ Γ(X.scheme, U) :=
  (X.constants U).toAlgebra

instance (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) : Nonempty (⊤ : X.scheme.Opens) :=
  ⟨⟨genericPoint X.scheme, by trivial⟩⟩

 
def functionFieldConstants (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) : ℂ →+* X.scheme.functionField :=
  (X.scheme.germToFunctionField ⊤).hom.comp X.globalConstants

instance (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) : Algebra ℂ X.scheme.functionField :=
  X.functionFieldConstants.toAlgebra

lemma germ_constants (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) (U : X.scheme.Opens) [Nonempty U] (c : ℂ) :
    algebraMap Γ(X.scheme, U) X.scheme.functionField (algebraMap ℂ Γ(X.scheme, U) c) =
      algebraMap ℂ X.scheme.functionField c := by
  change X.scheme.presheaf.germ U (genericPoint X.scheme) _
    (X.scheme.presheaf.map (homOfLE (le_top (a := U))).op (X.globalConstants c)) =
      X.scheme.presheaf.germ ⊤ (genericPoint X.scheme) _ (X.globalConstants c)
  exact X.scheme.presheaf.germ_res_apply (homOfLE le_top) (genericPoint X.scheme) _ _

instance (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) (U : X.scheme.Opens) [Nonempty U] :
    IsScalarTower ℂ Γ(X.scheme, U) X.scheme.functionField :=
  IsScalarTower.of_algebraMap_eq fun c => (X.germ_constants U c).symm

abbrev RationalCanonical (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) :=
  ⋀[X.scheme.functionField]^X.dimension (KaehlerDifferential ℂ X.scheme.functionField)

abbrev RationalPluriform (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) (m : ℕ) :=
  ⨂[X.scheme.functionField]^m X.RationalCanonical

instance (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) (m : ℕ) : AddCommGroup (X.RationalPluriform m) :=
  Module.addCommMonoidToAddCommGroup X.scheme.functionField

def regularPluriformLattice (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) (U : X.scheme.affineOpens)
    [Nonempty U.1] (m : ℕ) : Submodule Γ(X.scheme, U.1) (X.RationalPluriform m) :=
  Submodule.span Γ(X.scheme, U.1) <| Set.range fun a : Fin m → Fin X.dimension → Γ(X.scheme, U.1) =>
    PiTensorProduct.tprod X.scheme.functionField fun j =>
      exteriorPower.ιMulti X.scheme.functionField X.dimension fun i =>
        KaehlerDifferential.D ℂ X.scheme.functionField (algebraMap _ _ (a j i))

 

def IsCartierIdeal (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) (I : X.scheme.IdealSheafData) : Prop :=
  ∀ x : X.scheme, ∃ (U : X.scheme.affineOpens), x ∈ U.1 ∧
    ∃ t : Γ(X.scheme, U.1), t ≠ 0 ∧ I.ideal U = Ideal.span {t}

structure ReducedSNCBoundary (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) where
  count : ℕ
  components : Fin count → X.scheme.IdealSheafData
  cartier : ∀ i, X.IsCartierIdeal (components i)
  transverse : ∀ S : Finset (Fin count), S.Nonempty →
    IsEmpty ((S.sup components).subscheme) ∨
      (S.card ≤ X.dimension ∧ SmoothOfRelativeDimension (X.dimension - S.card)
        ((S.sup components).subschemeι ≫ X.structural))
  union_cartier : X.IsCartierIdeal (∏ i, components i)

namespace ReducedSNCBoundary

def ideal {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) : X.scheme.IdealSheafData :=
  ∏ i, E.components i

 
def support {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) : Set X.scheme :=
  E.ideal.support

def IsLogPluriform {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary)
    (m : ℕ) (s : X.RationalPluriform m) : Prop :=
  ∀ (U : X.scheme.affineOpens) (_ : Nonempty U.1) (t : Γ(X.scheme, U.1)),
    t ≠ 0 → E.ideal.ideal U = Ideal.span {t} →
      (algebraMap Γ(X.scheme, U.1) X.scheme.functionField t)^m • s ∈
        X.regularPluriformLattice U m

lemma isLogPluriform_zero {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) (m : ℕ) :
    E.IsLogPluriform m 0 := by
  intro U hU t ht hI
  simp only [smul_zero, Submodule.zero_mem]

lemma isLogPluriform_add {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) (m : ℕ)
    {s r : X.RationalPluriform m} (hs : E.IsLogPluriform m s) (hr : E.IsLogPluriform m r) :
    E.IsLogPluriform m (s + r) := by
  intro U hU t ht hI
  simpa only [smul_add] using (X.regularPluriformLattice U m).add_mem
    (hs U hU t ht hI) (hr U hU t ht hI)

lemma isLogPluriform_smul {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) (m : ℕ)
    (c : ℂ) {s : X.RationalPluriform m} (hs : E.IsLogPluriform m s) :
    E.IsLogPluriform m (c • s) := by
  intro U hU t ht hI
  have h := (X.regularPluriformLattice U m).smul_mem
    (algebraMap ℂ Γ(X.scheme, U.1) c) (hs U hU t ht hI)
  rw [IsScalarTower.algebraMap_smul Γ(X.scheme, U.1)] at h
  rwa [smul_comm c] at h

 

def sections {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) (m : ℕ) :
    Submodule ℂ (X.RationalPluriform m) where
  carrier := E.IsLogPluriform m
  zero_mem' := E.isLogPluriform_zero m
  add_mem' := E.isLogPluriform_add m
  smul_mem' := E.isLogPluriform_smul m

 

def HasImageDimensionAtLeast {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety}
    (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) (k : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ (m : ℕ), 0 < m ∧ ∃ s : X.RationalPluriform m,
    s ∈ E.sections m ∧ s ≠ 0 ∧ ∃ r : Fin k → X.scheme.functionField,
      AlgebraicIndependent ℂ r ∧ ∀ i, r i • s ∈ E.sections m

 

def kodaira {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) : WithBot ℕ∞ :=
  ⨆ (k : ℕ) (_ : E.HasImageDimensionAtLeast k), ((k : ℕ∞) : WithBot ℕ∞)

end ReducedSNCBoundary

 
structure ComplexPoint (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) where
  hom : complexBase ⟶ X.scheme
  over_base : hom ≫ X.structural = 𝟙 complexBase

 
def complexBasePoint : complexBase := ⟨⊥, Ideal.isPrime_bot⟩

 
def ComplexPoint.point {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (y : X.ComplexPoint) : X.scheme :=
  y.hom complexBasePoint

 
def ReducedSNCBoundary.complement {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) :
    X.scheme.Opens := E.ideal.support.compl

 

structure BoundaryFibration (X Y : SmoothProjectiveVariety)
    (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) (D : Y.ReducedSNCBoundary) where
  hom : X.scheme ⟶ Y.scheme
  over_base : hom ≫ Y.structural = X.structural
  surjective : Surjective hom
  connected : ∀ y : Y.ComplexPoint, ConnectedSpace ↥(CategoryTheory.Limits.pullback hom y.hom)
  support : hom ⁻¹' D.support ⊆ E.support

 

structure StratumSmoothFibration (X Y : SmoothProjectiveVariety)
    (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) (D : Y.ReducedSNCBoundary)
    extends BoundaryFibration X Y E D where
  smooth : Smooth (hom.resLE D.complement (hom ⁻¹ᵁ D.complement) le_rfl)
  strata : ∀ (S : Finset (Fin E.count)), S.Nonempty →
    Smooth (((S.sup E.components).subschemeι ≫ hom).resLE D.complement
      (((S.sup E.components).subschemeι ≫ hom) ⁻¹ᵁ D.complement) le_rfl)

 

structure FiberModel {X Y : SmoothProjectiveVariety} {E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary}
    {D : Y.ReducedSNCBoundary} (f : BoundaryFibration X Y E D) (y : Y.ComplexPoint) where
  variety : SmoothProjectiveVariety
  inclusion : variety.scheme ⟶ X.scheme
  square : IsPullback inclusion variety.structural f.hom y.hom
  boundary : variety.ReducedSNCBoundary
  boundary_pullback : boundary.ideal = E.ideal.comap inclusion

 

def VeryGenerally (Y : SmoothProjectiveVariety) (P : Y.ComplexPoint → Prop) : Prop :=
  ∃ Z : ℕ → TopologicalSpace.Closeds Y.scheme,
    (∀ n, Z n ≠ ⊤) ∧ ∀ y : Y.ComplexPoint, (∀ n, y.point ∉ Z n) → P y

end SmoothProjectiveVariety

end ReverseLogKodaira

namespace ReverseLogKodaira.SmoothProjectiveVariety

theorem veryGenerally_fiber_negative_branch
    {X Y : SmoothProjectiveVariety} {E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary} {D : Y.ReducedSNCBoundary}
    (f : StratumSmoothFibration X Y E D) :
    VeryGenerally Y fun y => y.point ∈ D.complement ∧
      ∀ F : FiberModel f.toBoundaryFibration y, F.boundary.kodaira = ⊥ →
        E.kodaira = D.kodaira + F.boundary.kodaira ∧
        ∀ (m : ℕ), 0 < m → ∀ s : X.RationalPluriform m, s ∈ E.sections m → s = 0 := by sorry

end ReverseLogKodaira.SmoothProjectiveVariety

end

end OAI
```

    {

    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "lemma",
      "name": "germ_constants",
      "statement_lean": "lemma germ_constants (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) (U : X.scheme.Opens) [Nonempty U] (c : ℂ) :\n    algebraMap Γ(X.scheme, U) X.scheme.functionField (algebraMap ℂ Γ(X.scheme, U) c) =\n      algebraMap ℂ X.scheme.functionField c := by\n  change X.scheme.presheaf.germ U (genericPoint X.scheme) _\n    (X.scheme.presheaf.map (homOfLE (le_top (a := U))).op (X.globalConstants c)) =\n      X.scheme.presheaf.germ ⊤ (genericPoint X.scheme) _ (X.globalConstants c)\n  exact X.scheme.presheaf.germ_res_apply (homOfLE le_top) (genericPoint X.scheme) _ _"
    },

      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "instance",

    },

      "docstring": null,

      "name": "RationalCanonical",
      "statement_lean": "abbrev RationalCanonical (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) :=\n  ⋀[X.scheme.functionField]^X.dimension (KaehlerDifferential ℂ X.scheme.functionField)"
    },

      "docstring": null,

    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "instance",
      "name": "instance",
      "statement_lean": "instance (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) (m : ℕ) : AddCommGroup (X.RationalPluriform m) :=\n  Module.addCommMonoidToAddCommGroup X.scheme.functionField"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "regularPluriformLattice",
      "statement_lean": "def regularPluriformLattice (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) (U : X.scheme.affineOpens)\n    [Nonempty U.1] (m : ℕ) : Submodule Γ(X.scheme, U.1) (X.RationalPluriform m) :=\n  Submodule.span Γ(X.scheme, U.1) <| Set.range fun a : Fin m → Fin X.dimension → Γ(X.scheme, U.1) =>\n    PiTensorProduct.tprod X.scheme.functionField fun j =>\n      exteriorPower.ιMulti X.scheme.functionField X.dimension fun i =>\n        KaehlerDifferential.D ℂ X.scheme.functionField (algebraMap _ _ (a j i))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "IsCartierIdeal",
      "statement_lean": "def IsCartierIdeal (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) (I : X.scheme.IdealSheafData) : Prop :=\n  ∀ x : X.scheme, ∃ (U : X.scheme.affineOpens), x ∈ U.1 ∧\n    ∃ t : Γ(X.scheme, U.1), t ≠ 0 ∧ I.ideal U = Ideal.span {t}"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "ReducedSNCBoundary",
      "statement_lean": "structure ReducedSNCBoundary (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) where\n  count : ℕ\n  components : Fin count → X.scheme.IdealSheafData\n  cartier : ∀ i, X.IsCartierIdeal (components i)\n  transverse : ∀ S : Finset (Fin count), S.Nonempty →\n    IsEmpty ((S.sup components).subscheme) ∨\n      (S.card ≤ X.dimension ∧ SmoothOfRelativeDimension (X.dimension - S.card)\n        ((S.sup components).subschemeι ≫ X.structural))\n  union_cartier : X.IsCartierIdeal (∏ i, components i)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "ideal",
      "statement_lean": "def ideal {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) : X.scheme.IdealSheafData :=\n  ∏ i, E.components i"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "support",
      "statement_lean": "def support {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) : Set X.scheme :=\n  E.ideal.support"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "IsLogPluriform",
      "statement_lean": "def IsLogPluriform {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary)\n    (m : ℕ) (s : X.RationalPluriform m) : Prop :=\n  ∀ (U : X.scheme.affineOpens) (_ : Nonempty U.1) (t : Γ(X.scheme, U.1)),\n    t ≠ 0 → E.ideal.ideal U = Ideal.span {t} →\n      (algebraMap Γ(X.scheme, U.1) X.scheme.functionField t)^m • s ∈\n        X.regularPluriformLattice U m"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "lemma",
      "name": "isLogPluriform_zero",
      "statement_lean": "lemma isLogPluriform_zero {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) (m : ℕ) :\n    E.IsLogPluriform m 0 := by\n  intro U hU t ht hI\n  simp only [smul_zero, Submodule.zero_mem]"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "lemma",
      "name": "isLogPluriform_add",
      "statement_lean": "lemma isLogPluriform_add {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) (m : ℕ)\n    {s r : X.RationalPluriform m} (hs : E.IsLogPluriform m s) (hr : E.IsLogPluriform m r) :\n    E.IsLogPluriform m (s + r) := by\n  intro U hU t ht hI\n  simpa only [smul_add] using (X.regularPluriformLattice U m).add_mem\n    (hs U hU t ht hI) (hr U hU t ht hI)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "lemma",
      "name": "isLogPluriform_smul",
      "statement_lean": "lemma isLogPluriform_smul {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) (m : ℕ)\n    (c : ℂ) {s : X.RationalPluriform m} (hs : E.IsLogPluriform m s) :\n    E.IsLogPluriform m (c • s) := by\n  intro U hU t ht hI\n  have h := (X.regularPluriformLattice U m).smul_mem\n    (algebraMap ℂ Γ(X.scheme, U.1) c) (hs U hU t ht hI)\n  rw [IsScalarTower.algebraMap_smul Γ(X.scheme, U.1)] at h\n  rwa [smul_comm c] at h"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "sections",
      "statement_lean": "def sections {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) (m : ℕ) :\n    Submodule ℂ (X.RationalPluriform m) where\n  carrier := E.IsLogPluriform m\n  zero_mem' := E.isLogPluriform_zero m\n  add_mem' := E.isLogPluriform_add m\n  smul_mem' := E.isLogPluriform_smul m"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "HasImageDimensionAtLeast",
      "statement_lean": "def HasImageDimensionAtLeast {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety}\n    (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) (k : ℕ) : Prop :=\n  ∃ (m : ℕ), 0 < m ∧ ∃ s : X.RationalPluriform m,\n    s ∈ E.sections m ∧ s ≠ 0 ∧ ∃ r : Fin k → X.scheme.functionField,\n      AlgebraicIndependent ℂ r ∧ ∀ i, r i • s ∈ E.sections m"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "kodaira",
      "statement_lean": "def kodaira {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) : WithBot ℕ∞ :=\n  ⨆ (k : ℕ) (_ : E.HasImageDimensionAtLeast k), ((k : ℕ∞) : WithBot ℕ∞)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",

      "statement_lean": "structure ComplexPoint (X : SmoothProjectiveVariety) where\n  hom : complexBase ⟶ X.scheme\n  over_base : hom ≫ X.structural = 𝟙 complexBase"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,

      "statement_lean": "def complexBasePoint : complexBase := ⟨⊥, Ideal.isPrime_bot⟩"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "ComplexPoint.point",
      "statement_lean": "def ComplexPoint.point {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (y : X.ComplexPoint) : X.scheme :=\n  y.hom complexBasePoint"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "ReducedSNCBoundary.complement",
      "statement_lean": "def ReducedSNCBoundary.complement {X : SmoothProjectiveVariety} (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) :\n    X.scheme.Opens := E.ideal.support.compl"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "BoundaryFibration",
      "statement_lean": "structure BoundaryFibration (X Y : SmoothProjectiveVariety)\n    (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) (D : Y.ReducedSNCBoundary) where\n  hom : X.scheme ⟶ Y.scheme\n  over_base : hom ≫ Y.structural = X.structural\n  surjective : Surjective hom\n  connected : ∀ y : Y.ComplexPoint, ConnectedSpace ↥(CategoryTheory.Limits.pullback hom y.hom)\n  support : hom ⁻¹' D.support ⊆ E.support"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "StratumSmoothFibration",
      "statement_lean": "structure StratumSmoothFibration (X Y : SmoothProjectiveVariety)\n    (E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary) (D : Y.ReducedSNCBoundary)\n    extends BoundaryFibration X Y E D where\n  smooth : Smooth (hom.resLE D.complement (hom ⁻¹ᵁ D.complement) le_rfl)\n  strata : ∀ (S : Finset (Fin E.count)), S.Nonempty →\n    Smooth (((S.sup E.components).subschemeι ≫ hom).resLE D.complement\n      (((S.sup E.components).subschemeι ≫ hom) ⁻¹ᵁ D.complement) le_rfl)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "FiberModel",
      "statement_lean": "structure FiberModel {X Y : SmoothProjectiveVariety} {E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary}\n    {D : Y.ReducedSNCBoundary} (f : BoundaryFibration X Y E D) (y : Y.ComplexPoint) where\n  variety : SmoothProjectiveVariety\n  inclusion : variety.scheme ⟶ X.scheme\n  square : IsPullback inclusion variety.structural f.hom y.hom\n  boundary : variety.ReducedSNCBoundary\n  boundary_pullback : boundary.ideal = E.ideal.comap inclusion"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "VeryGenerally",
      "statement_lean": "def VeryGenerally (Y : SmoothProjectiveVariety) (P : Y.ComplexPoint → Prop) : Prop :=\n  ∃ Z : ℕ → TopologicalSpace.Closeds Y.scheme,\n    (∀ n, Z n ≠ ⊤) ∧ ∀ y : Y.ComplexPoint, (∀ n, y.point ∉ Z n) → P y"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "theorem",
      "name": "veryGenerally_fiber_negative_branch",
      "statement_lean": "theorem veryGenerally_fiber_negative_branch\n    {X Y : SmoothProjectiveVariety} {E : X.ReducedSNCBoundary} {D : Y.ReducedSNCBoundary}\n    (f : StratumSmoothFibration X Y E D) :\n    VeryGenerally Y fun y => y.point ∈ D.complement ∧\n      ∀ F : FiberModel f.toBoundaryFibration y, F.boundary.kodaira = ⊥ →\n        E.kodaira = D.kodaira + F.boundary.kodaira ∧\n        ∀ (m : ℕ), 0 < m → ∀ s : X.RationalPluriform m, s ∈ E.sections m → s = 0 := by sorry"
    }
  ],

  "schema": "pm.formal.v2",
  "scope": {
    "doc": "lean/docs/033.md",
    "family": "033",
    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [The reverse logarithmic Kodaira inequality and additivity](../../preprints/The-reverse-logarithmic-Kodaira-inequality-and-additivity-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nThe paper studies logarithmic Kodaira additivity for connected-fiber morphisms of smooth projective reduced simple-normal-crossing pairs that are smooth on all boundary strata away from the base boundary. The linked formalization proves the negative-fiber branch: for a very general base point, if the logarithmic Kodaira dimension of the fiber is $-\\infty$, then the total logarithmic Kodaira dimension equals the sum of the base and fiber dimensions and is $-\\infty$. Every positive-degree logarithmic pluriform section on the total space then vanishes.\n\nThe finite-dimension and negative-base branches of the paper's additivity theorem are outside this selected statement."
  },
  "status": "present",

}

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the reverse logarithmic Kodaira inequality for a surjective connected-fiber morphism $`f:(X,E)\to(Y,D)`$ of smooth projective reduced simple-normal-crossing pairs, with $`\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\nolimits (f^*D)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\nolimits E`$, such that <i>X</i> and every boundary stratum are smooth over $`Y\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\nolimits D`$. Together with logarithmic subadditivity, the inequality gives additivity, including both negative-infinity cases. This resolves Popa's logarithmic additivity conjecture positively in the projective reduced-SNC, stratum-smooth setting.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let a smooth family be completed to a morphism of projective pairs. How many logarithmic pluricanonical forms can its total space have? Subadditivity gives a lower bound from the base and a general fiber. We prove the complementary upper bound when the total space and all boundary strata are smooth over the open base. The boundary may have horizontal components, and the conclusion includes the case in which the base has no logarithmic pluricanonical forms at all.

<a id="eq:support-inclusion"></a>
Throughout, varieties are over $\mathbb C$. Let $f:X\to Y$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibers between smooth connected projective varieties. Let $E$ and $D$ be reduced simple-normal-crossing divisors on $X$ and $Y$, respectively, with $$\label{eq:support-inclusion}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}(f^*D)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}(E).$$ Either divisor may be zero. Set $V=Y\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}D$. We assume that $X$ and every irreducible component of every nonempty intersection of components of $E$, restricted to $f^{-1}(V)$, are smooth over $V$. We call this *stratum smoothness over $V$*. For a very general $y\in V$, set $F=X_y$ and $E_F=E|_F$. Write $$L_X=K_X+E,\qquad L_Y=K_Y+D,\qquad L_F=K_F+E_F.$$ We give a point Kodaira dimension zero and use $(-\infty)+a=-\infty$, also when $a=-\infty$.

<a id="stmt-thm-upper-bound"></a>
<div id="thm:upper-bound" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Reverse logarithmic Kodaira inequality). *Under these hypotheses, <a id="eq:upper-bound"></a>$$\label{eq:upper-bound}
 \kappa(X,L_X)\leq\kappa(Y,L_Y)+\kappa(F,L_F).$$ If either term on the right is $-\infty$, then $H^0(X,mL_X)=0$ for every $m>0$.*

</div>

There is no abundance, semiampleness, good-minimal-model, or general-type hypothesis. Condition <a href="#eq:support-inclusion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:support-inclusion">[eq:support-inclusion]</a> is a condition on supports; the multiplicities in $f^*D$ remain in the proof. No smoothness is assumed over $D$.

The additional input for equality is the logarithmic subadditivity theorem of the companion article (OpenAI 2026, Corollary 6.2) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)]. It applies to any connected-fiber surjective morphism of smooth projective reduced-SNC pairs satisfying <a href="#eq:support-inclusion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:support-inclusion">[eq:support-inclusion]</a>, without the stratum-smoothness assumption. Its fiber is the same very general pair $(F,E_F)$, and it gives the opposite inequality. Therefore:

<a id="stmt-cor-additivity"></a>
<div id="cor:additivity" class="corollary">

<a id="eq:additivity"></a>
**Corollary 2** (Logarithmic additivity). *For the pairs in Theorem <a href="#thm:upper-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:upper-bound">1</a>, $$\label{eq:additivity}
 \kappa(X,K_X+E)=\kappa(Y,K_Y+D)+\kappa(F,K_F+E_F).$$*

</div>

This gives a positive resolution of Popa’s logarithmic additivity conjecture in its projective reduced-SNC, stratum-smooth formulation (Popa 2025, Conjecture 3.9 and its defining footnote) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[PopaSurvey](#ref-PopaSurvey)]. The proof of the upper inequality is independent of logarithmic subadditivity; the companion theorem is used only for this corollary.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The section-construction result

The difficult case is a base with logarithmic Kodaira dimension zero. The space of logarithmic pluricanonical sections then has dimension at most one in each degree. Choose a very general point outside the zero divisors of all these sections. If a total-space pluriform vanished on its fiber, we would like to produce a base pluriform vanishing at that point. This would be a contradiction, and restriction to the fiber would be injective in every degree.

The following result carries out that construction. It also treats a base of logarithmic Kodaira dimension $-\infty$.

<a id="stmt-prop-section-construction"></a>
<div id="prop:section-construction" class="proposition">

**Proposition 3**. *Let $f,E,D$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:upper-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:upper-bound">1</a>, and let $0\ne s\in H^0(X,mL_X)$ for some $m>0$. Then $\kappa(Y,L_Y)\geq0$. If $\kappa(Y,L_Y)=0$ and $s$ vanishes identically on $X_y$ for some $y\in V$, there are an integer $a>0$ and a nonzero $\tau\in H^0(Y,aL_Y)$ with $\tau(y)=0$.*

</div>

The point $y$ in this statement belongs to the original open base $V$. It need not belong to a smaller open set chosen in constructing a cyclic cover from $s$. This uniformity is what allows the restriction argument to handle all total-space sections at one very general fiber. In particular, if $\kappa(Y,L_Y)=0$, restriction to one very general fiber is injective in every positive degree, so $$h^0(X,mL_X)\leq h^0(F,mL_F)
 \qquad\text{for every positive integer }m.$$ [sec:completion](#sec:completion) proves [thm:upper-bound](#thm:upper-bound) from this proposition, including both negative-infinity cases.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Two outcomes of the Hodge construction

A pluriform on the total space first produces a cohomology class: take a root of the pluriform on a finite cover, then project its class to a nonzero pure weight quotient. This gives a vector in the bundle of highest Hodge classes. The chosen vector need not span that bundle. Thus positivity of the determinant of the whole bundle does not yet control the coordinates of this particular vector, or retain its zero on a chosen fiber.

We keep track of these coordinates in the logarithmic differential frames of the original pair $(Y,D)$. The root cover may acquire extra singular fibers inside $V$, but its new discriminant must not enlarge the poles allowed on the base. A local calculation shows both that no new poles occur and that a zero on any original smooth fiber is retained. Homogeneous tensor operations preserve these properties while making the cohomological data descend to a projective parameter space $S$. This space records the varying Hodge structure and the additional vector directions that its period map alone can miss. There are two possibilities.

If $S$ is a point, the resulting cohomology bundle is constant. Its vector has ordinary scalar coordinates, at least one of which is nonzero. The coefficient calculation makes each coordinate an actual base pluriform. The same calculation preserves the prescribed zero. This proves base nonvanishing, excluding a base of logarithmic Kodaira dimension $-\infty$. The preserved zero gives the additional conclusion needed when its logarithmic Kodaira dimension is zero.

If $S$ has positive dimension, restrict first to the generic fiber of the map from the base to $S$. The coefficient system is constant there, so a nonzero scalar coordinate permits a relative Iitaka fibration. It produces a divisor on an intermediate variety with the same sufficiently divisible section spaces as $K_Y+D$. Period positivity makes this divisor big after adding a pullback from $S$. The remaining obstacle is to remove that added divisor. We do so by comparing the iterated Higgs images of the chosen vector with the period directions and applying a numerical subtraction argument. The divisor itself is then big, which forces $\kappa(Y,K_Y+D)>0$. Thus this alternative cannot occur when the base has logarithmic Kodaira dimension zero or $-\infty$.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Earlier results and the proof route

Iitaka’s subadditivity problem asks whether the Kodaira dimension of an algebraic fiber space is at least the sum of those of its base and general fiber. Viehweg proved the relative-dimension-one case (Viehweg 1977, Introduction) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Viehweg1977](#ref-Viehweg1977)]. Logarithmic pluriforms bring open varieties into the same birational framework (Iitaka 1976, Introduction) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[IitakaLogForms1976](#ref-IitakaLogForms1976)].

For smooth families, the stronger question is whether the lower bound becomes equality. Popa’s logarithmic formulation specifies smoothness of the total space and every boundary stratum over the open base (Popa 2025, Conjecture 3.9 and footnote 5) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[PopaSurvey](#ref-PopaSurvey)]. The reverse inequality constrains the extra pluricanonical growth that variation might otherwise produce. Its negative-base case makes the distinction from subadditivity especially clear: the lower bound is then automatic, whereas the upper bound asserts vanishing of every positive pluricanonical space on the total pair.

Popa–Schnell proved the upper inequality for smooth projective families under the Campana–Peternell conjecture and obtained a degreewise plurigenus bound when the base has rationally trivial canonical bundle (Popa and Schnell 2023, Theorems C and H) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[PopaSchnellSmooth](#ref-PopaSchnellSmooth)]. Their reduction through the base Iitaka fibration also organizes our argument. The section-construction proposition adds the specific conclusion needed here: an actual base pluriform, with a prescribed zero whenever the original section vanishes on a chosen smooth fiber.

Fujino–Fujisawa’s logarithmic results already allow relatively normal-crossing horizontal boundary. Their upper inequality for a base of nonnegative logarithmic Kodaira dimension assumes generalized abundance for sufficiently general fibers of the base’s logarithmic Iitaka fibration (Fujino and Fujisawa 2024, Theorem 1.12) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[FF](#ref-FF)]; their Hodge-theoretic construction also descends pseudoeffectivity to the base (Fujino and Fujisawa 2024, Theorems 1.4 and 4.5) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[FF](#ref-FF)]. Park proves the logarithmic upper inequality, in the reduced inverse-image boundary setting and for a base of nonnegative logarithmic Kodaira dimension, assuming good minimal models for the very general fibers of that base Iitaka fibration (Park 2026, Theorem 1.12) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Park](#ref-Park)]. These hypotheses concern fibers of the *base’s* Iitaka fibration, rather than the original fibers of $f$.

A different route is Campana’s logarithmic additivity theorem for smooth projective families over a quasiprojective base with semiample canonical bundles on the original fibers (Campana 2023, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[CampanaAdditivity](#ref-CampanaAdditivity)]. Its proof uses birational isotriviality and the core. The extension to original fibers with good minimal models uses Taji’s birational-isotriviality theorem over a special base (Taji 2023, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Taji](#ref-Taji)], as explained in (Campana 2023, Remark 2(1)) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[CampanaAdditivity](#ref-CampanaAdditivity)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:upper-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:upper-bound">1</a> imposes neither of these minimal-model conditions.

The differential construction has an earlier model in Kovács’s iteration of cohomology maps from logarithmic cotangent sequences (Kovács 2000, sec. 1, Lemma 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[KovacsFineModuli](#ref-KovacsFineModuli)]. The passage from a pluriform to a cyclic cover and iterated Higgs maps belongs to the Viehweg–Zuo method (Viehweg and Zuo 2001, Introduction, Lemma 1.1, and Section 3) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[ViehwegZuoIsotriviality](#ref-ViehwegZuoIsotriviality)]. We use its Hodge-theoretic development in Popa–Schnell and the logarithmic construction of Fujino–Fujisawa, while retaining the chosen vector and its original coefficient lattice. Further inputs are full integral period-image algebraicity (Bakker, Brunebarbe, and Tsimerman 2023) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[BBT](#ref-BBT)], orbifold cotangent positivity (Campana and Păun 2019) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[CP](#ref-CP)], and b-nefness of the moduli part of an lc-trivial fibration (Fujino and Gongyo 2014) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[FG](#ref-FG)]. For the last input we verify the rank condition for an auxiliary subpair which may have negative coefficients. The new task addressed by the section construction is the passage from these positivity statements to actual base sections with the stated control at every point of the original open base.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### Organization.

[sec:completion](#sec:completion) deduces the upper inequality in all sign cases from [prop:section-construction](#prop:section-construction). The rest of the paper proves that proposition. [sec:logarithmic-input](#sec:logarithmic-input) constructs the geometric vector, its coefficient lattice, and the zero test. [sec:marked-periods](#sec:marked-periods) constructs the quotient that retains its periods and compact-factor directions. [sec:boundary-rank](#sec:boundary-rank) proves the two comparisons that remove the boundary and control the chosen vector by its iterated Higgs images. [sec:relative-iitaka](#sec:relative-iitaka) produces an intermediate base with exactly the original logarithmic section spaces. [sec:nonvanishing](#sec:nonvanishing) first proves the constant-parameter case, then removes the period twist in the positive-dimensional case and completes the section construction. Metric and flat-connection estimates appear immediately before their first uses.

<a id="sec:completion"></a>
### Reduction to the section construction

The sole input from the remaining sections is [prop:section-construction](#prop:section-construction). We first give the reduction in every sign case. This identifies exactly what the geometric construction must produce, including its assertion at a prescribed point of the original open base.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Conventions and birational changes

All varieties are over $\mathbb C$, except when a generic fiber is explicitly considered over a characteristic-zero function field. We use additive notation for line bundles and rational Cartier divisors. A statement about sections of a rational divisor is understood after taking a positive Cartier multiple. For numerical divisor classes, $P\ge_{\mathrm{pe}}Q$ means that $P-Q$ is pseudoeffective.

We make projective resolutions and resolve rational maps throughout. Resolution may be taken to preserve a specified smooth open set and to be equivariant for a finite group; see (Kollár 2007, Theorems 3.35 and 3.36) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[KollarResolution](#ref-KollarResolution)]. Here is the boundary convention that accompanies a birational modification. If $\mu:Z'\to Z$ is a birational morphism between smooth projective varieties and $\Delta$ is reduced SNC, put $$\Delta'=\mu^{-1}_*\Delta+\operatorname{Exc}(\mu)_{\mathrm{red}},$$ after further resolution when necessary. The log canonicity of the smooth pair $(Z,\Delta)$ gives <a id="eq:prelim-birational-log"></a>$$\label{eq:prelim-birational-log}
 K_{Z'}+\Delta'=\mu^*(K_Z+\Delta)+A_\mu,
 \qquad A_\mu\geq0\quad\text{$\mu$-exceptional}.$$ Consequently pullback identifies the spaces of logarithmic pluricanonical sections in every degree. Indeed, pullback supplies one inclusion; in the other direction a rational section regular away from a set of codimension at least two on the smooth variety $Z$ extends across that set. Equivalently, $\mu_*\mathcal O_{Z'}(mA_\mu)=\mathcal O_Z$. This convention also applies to a generic fiber after a characteristic-zero extension of the ground field.

<a id="eq:prelim-numerical-dimension"></a>
We use the standard numerical properties of big and nef divisors (Lazarsfeld 2004, vol. 48, chaps. 1–2) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Lazarsfeld](#ref-Lazarsfeld)]. In particular, the big cone is the interior of the pseudoeffective cone, and adding a pseudoeffective class to a big class preserves bigness. If $P$ is nef on an $n$-dimensional projective variety, its numerical dimension is $$\label{eq:prelim-numerical-dimension}
 \nu(P)=\max\{k\in\{0,\ldots,n\}:P^k A^{n-k}>0\},
 \qquad A\ \text{ample},$$ independently of $A$, and $P$ is big exactly when $\nu(P)=n$. Bigness is preserved in both directions by a dominant generically finite map. For descent, factor the map through its finite normalization and apply the field norm to an effective multiple of the pulled-back divisor minus a sufficiently small pullback of an ample divisor. Birational invariance then gives the assertion on a resolution. On a point the empty intersection product is $1$, and numerical and Iitaka dimensions of the zero line are both zero.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The two negative-infinity cases

<span id="proof:fiber-vanishing" label="proof:fiber-vanishing"></span> Suppose first that $\kappa(F,L_F)=-\infty$. A nonzero section of $mL_X$ cannot vanish identically on every very general fiber of a dominant map: its nonvanishing locus is a nonempty open subset of $X$, whose image contains a dense open subset of $Y$. Restriction and adjunction would therefore give a nonzero section of $mL_F$ on a very general fiber. This is impossible. Hence $\kappa(X,L_X)=-\infty$.

<span id="proof:base-vanishing" label="proof:base-vanishing"></span> Suppose next that $\kappa(Y,L_Y)=-\infty$. A hypothetical nonzero section of $mL_X$ contradicts the first assertion of [prop:section-construction](#prop:section-construction). Thus the total Kodaira dimension is again $-\infty$. This is an actual vanishing conclusion, not a consequence of an automatic lower inequality.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### A base of Kodaira dimension zero

Assume $\kappa(Y,L_Y)=0$. If $Y$ is a point, the conclusion is immediate. Otherwise choose $y\in V$ very general so that all fiber pluriform spaces have their very general dimensions and $y$ lies outside the zero divisor of every nonzero base pluriform. This last condition is possible: <a id="eq:base-one-section"></a>$$\label{eq:base-one-section}
 h^0(Y,aL_Y)\leq 1\qquad(a>0).$$ Indeed, two linearly independent sections in a common degree have a nonconstant ratio and hence give Iitaka dimension at least one. There are only countably many degrees to consider.
